ParlaMint-PL_2016-07-20-sejm-23-2.u4	Thank you very much. Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! When I present my report on the implementation of the State budget in 2015, I would like to start by presenting the conditions for the implementation of the budget in this year. From today's perspective, we can conclude that the 2015 budget was implemented in relatively favourable macroeconomic conditions. GDP growth in fixed prices was 3.6% and was higher than assumed by 0,2 percentage points. It was also almost twice as high as in the European Union, where it amounted to around 2%. However, it is worth taking into account the high share of the shadow economy, which is not part of GDP. On the basis of data from the Central Statistical Office, the Ministry of Finance and the Market Economy Research Institute, it can be concluded that the share of the grey area in the adjusted GDP was as high as 19,2% in 2015. However, the pace of growth, as well as the dynamics of budgetary revenue, was so unstable last year that the new government, having worrying data from the implementation of the budget for three quarters, and only in some cases in 11 months' time, was forced to revise the budget. The purpose of this amendment was to safeguard against the negative effects of much lower than planned tax revenues, especially VAT receipts. In addition, the aim was to reduce and implement expenditure due to, inter alia, to the proposed blockades and reductions of transfers, as well as the security of resources for the common agricultural policy. As we approximate the conditions for the implementation of the last year's budget, we can remind you that GDP growth has risen from approx. 3% in the first half of the year to more than 4% in the last quarter. This was mainly due to an accelerated domestic demand, which was accompanied by the continuation of a favourable trade balance. During the first half of the year, fast-growing investment expenditures played a major role in growth, while in the second half of the year consumption. However, on an annual basis, GDP components such as investment, domestic demand and imports, and higher public consumption and exports, have proved to be worse than those adopted for budget construction. The economic situation of Poland during 2015 proved to be quite favourable, although it was accompanied by uncertainty about the development of the situation throughout Europe and around the world. The continuation of the Ukrainian crisis, along with other worrying signals for investors, including in particular the dangerous development of the situation in the Middle East and the destabilising Europe of the refugee crisis, led to increased demand for safe currencies, especially for the dollar and Swiss franc, and consequently to strengthening these currencies in relation to emerging currencies, including the gold ones. The growing concern also caused the risk of explosions of the so-called. the third phase of the global financial crisis related to corporate over-indebtedness in parts of developing countries. In particular, the economic climate in Europe, especially in the case of export-based German economy, has had a strong negative impact on the economic downturn in China since many quarters. Against the background of global uncertainty, the economic performance shown in Poland in 2015 can therefore be considered rather satisfactory. It also shows that production has been steadily increasing and that the unemployment rate has fallen to an unlisted one-digit level since years. Good results were recorded in exports, with moderate import dynamics. This reduced the current account deficit to a very low level - 0.2% GDP - reducing Poland's exposure to possible disturbances in global financial flows. At the same time, the state of public finances has been improved and Poland has managed to reduce the public sector deficit below the 3% required by the EU treaties for the first time since the global crisis. GDP, although this was mainly due to the transfer of funds from OFE, as will be further mentioned. During the whole of 2015 there was a slight deflation, but resulting mainly not from the lack of money on the market, but from low world commodity prices. This phenomenon was probably objectively beneficial - I am talking about the prices of raw materials - for the Polish economy, but it caused perturbation in mining. Deflation led the National Bank of Poland to lower interest rates to historically low levels, as well as forced the government to take action to restructure mining. A clear negative financial shock was the sharp weakening of the golden course against the franc in January 2015, resulting from the change in the exchange rate policy of the Swiss National Bank. To sum up, despite the moderately favorable global situation, Poland's economic situation developed in 2015 in a rather favourable way, facilitating the implementation of the budget. This was supported mainly by the dynamics of domestic demand. However, a factor that hindered the situation was much longer than the expectations of deflation. When assessing the overall situation of Polish public finances in 2015, it should be noted first of all that during the year it was possible to reduce the scale of government and local government deficits below the 3% treaty limit. GDP, which was also a follow-up to financial recovery. On 19 June 2015, the Ecofin Council adopted a decision terminating the excessive deficit procedure for Poland, which reduced the excessive deficit one year earlier than recommended by the Council. The completion of the procedure and the theoretical improvement of the financial situation were directly linked to the acquisition by the government of PLN 153 billion in 2014, of assets from open pension funds transferred to the Social Security Office. The transfer resulted in a reduction in the 2014 deficit by PLN 23 billion in connection with a reduction in public debt expenditure and a reduction in transfers to the Social Insurance Fund. The acquisition of funds from OFE also resulted in a direct reduction of the public debt by PLN 130 billion. However, these achievements should not obscure two serious problems for years before Polish public finances. The first of these problems is due to the fact that in 2015 the fundamental systemic weakness of public finances, resulting from the very high share of rigid expenditure and the unfavourable structure of budgetary expenditure, i.e. the dominance of current and consumer expenditure over developmental expenditure, was not overcome. The second problem concerns the fact that, despite the fact that Poland has taken the excessive deficit procedure and the deficit reduction below the accepted criterion, due to the degree of imbalance in public finances, it is still impossible to ensure that the fiscal standards in force in the Economic and Monetary Union continue to be met in a sustainable manner. This can be demonstrated, among others. that, in the latest update of the convergence programme, the government assumed an increase in the structural deficit in 2016 to 3,1% GDP of 2,3% GDP in 2015 and an increase in general government deficit of 2.6% GDP of up to 2.9% this year GDP in 2017 and debt growth from 51.3% GDP in 2015 to 52.5% GDP in 2017, with the European Commission's forecasts even more pessimistic in this respect. In addition, the Supreme Audit Chamber points out that the rise in 2015 of the State's public debt and sovereign debt to comparable levels in 2013 shows that the effects of the change in the pension system in 2014 have been lost, which may also make it difficult to meet fiscal standards on a sustainable basis. The return of public debt to the level before the transfer of assets from OFE indicates that pension funds have been consumed by the State. In summary, the transfer of funds from OFE did not improve the financial situation, only increased the indicators for the period in which it took place. In conclusion, it should be noted that Poland in 2015 met the fiscal criteria of economic and monetary union for the first time in years, which should be considered a positive phenomenon regardless of the circumstances I have just mentioned. On the other hand, there are still weaknesses in Poland's public finances, and therefore the process of public finance reform should continue, and efforts to consolidate and improve the structure of Polish public finances are still necessary. When discussing revenue and expenditure and the balance of the budget in 2015, it can be concluded that tax revenues have been significantly lower than those originally established in the budget, as much as PLN 10.1 billion, i.e. by 3.8% , which resulted from a clear overestimation of VAT revenues, up to PLN 11.5 billion, i.e. 8,5% the total revenue assumed. Much lower than the assumed VAT revenue was the most serious problem in the implementation of the budget. To show its scale, I will compare the situation of this year with that of 2014, when VAT revenues amounted to PLN 124 262 million. In 2015, they amounted to PLN 123 120 million. The decrease in these revenues was therefore recorded by almost PLN 1 billion. However, the greatest impact on the decrease in VAT revenue is due to the persistence of a negative trend related to its fraud. According to the Supreme Audit Chamber, tax authorities detected fictional invoices for 2013 for a total amount of PLN 19.7 billion, already PLN 33.7 billion in 2014 and as much as PLN 81.9 billion in 2015. Moreover, the low efficiency of the recovery of the tax due resulted in a sharp increase in VAT arrears, from PLN 5.1 billion at the end of 2013 to PLN 14.1 billion at the end of June 2015. In the assessment of the Supreme Audit Chamber, the possibilities for recovery of these amounts are limited. It should therefore be pointed out that a serious gap in VAT receipts has persisted in 2015 to show that the measures taken to improve its collection during that period have proved insufficient. The Supreme Chamber of Control has called for, among others. to improve the situation in this respect, the reorganisation of tax services and the creation of a central computer register of VAT invoices. These actions were not taken in 2015, but were announced by the government in 2016. [[They've been taken.]] The forecast of other budget revenue outside VAT was generally correct except for slightly overestimated excise revenue. The remaining revenue exceeded the expected revenue, in particular the revenue of the budgetary units, despite the transfer to 2016 of auction payments to the LTE frequency reservation. This allowed for a partial complement to the VAT deficit. As a result, total budget revenue amounted to PLN 289.1 billion in 2015 and turned out to be PLN 8.1 billion, i.e. 2.7% , lower than that provided for in the original budget law and by PLN 2.4 billion, i.e. 0.8% , higher than that provided for in the amendment of this law. The expenditure of the state budget amounted to PLN 331.7 billion, i.e. significantly lower than that of the original law, as much as PLN 11.6 billion, i.e. 3.4% , and lower than foreseen in the amended act by PLN 4.9 billion, i.e. 1.5%. This was achieved mainly by reducing the expenditure of current budgetary units and expenditure on co-financing Union programmes, as well as by lower than projected expenditure on public debt management. As a result, the deficit amounted to PLN 42.6 billion in 2015. It turned out to be lower by PLN 3.5 billion, i.e. 7.7% , compared to the original law and by PLN 7.4 billion, i.e. by 14,8% , lower than the deficit provided for in the amended act. When assessing the implementation of revenue and expenditure in relation to the initial and revised budget law for 2015, it must be concluded that it has been carried out with distortions in relation to that provided for in the original act due to the significant revaluation of VAT receipts, and that this implementation has been satisfactory in relation to the amended act. Thanks to the savings achieved on the expenditure side, it was possible not to exceed the deficit level for both the original and the revised law. The implementation of the basic size of the budget shows that the 2015 budget implementation report deserves to be adopted. In quite favourable economic conditions, despite problems with the implementation of the assumed VAT revenue, the savings on the expenditure side managed to keep the budget deficit below the level assumed both in the original and in the revised budget law. I will move on to other aspects of budget implementation. The expenditure of the budget of European funds was realized in 2015 in the amount of PLN 67.7 billion. They accounted for 97.9% the plan after the changes. Compared to the 2014 performance, they were lower by 1%. In 2015, the Council Poland has received PLN 65.4 billion from the European Union budget, while PLN 18.2 billion has been paid to the European Union budget. This means that the Polish economy gained net funds of PLN 47.2 billion. Among the new Member States of the European Union, Poland remained the main recipient of direct payments under the common agricultural policy and was the largest beneficiary of cohesion policy and the Rural Development Programme. The implementation of selected strategic objectives identified, inter alia, in the long-term development strategy of the country. Poland 2030 and the country 2020 strategy, whose assumptions are correlated with the European Europe 2020 programming document and nine integrated strategies, have been satisfactory, albeit in a diverse way. For example, the European Commission has assessed that, within the framework of the national targets set under the Europe 2020 strategy, Poland has achieved good results in the areas of reducing greenhouse gas emissions, energy efficiency and higher education, while pointing out that greater efforts are needed to increase employment rates, investment in R &amp; D, renewable energy, reducing early school leaving and reducing poverty. With regard to the long-term development strategy of the country. Poland 2030. The third wave of modernity and the country's 2020 strategy for development, the lack of publication on the value of most indicators of their implementation in 2015 prevented an assessment of the current state of implementation. However, the data presented indicated that achieving the objectives envisaged in the strategy could not be realistic. Nevertheless, the long time horizon of implementation offers an opportunity to achieve results similar to those planned. In 2015 a limit of 200 billion PLN guarantees and guarantees was set in the Budget Act. This limit was used in the amount of approx. PLN 8.5 billion, i.e. 4,3%, which should be assessed positively in view of the preventive nature of the above measures to safeguard the potential use of the Treasury guarantee in order to support those at risk of possible consequences of the financial and economic crisis. Compared to 2014, the use of the State Treasury guarantees and guarantees was lower, but also the threat of the crisis was clearly weaker. The State Treasury's potential non-reliability on guarantees and guarantees at the end of 2015 amounted to PLN 113.2 billion. This meant that they increased by PLN 2.4 billion compared to 2014. The largest amount of guarantees and guarantees was used by Bank Gospodarstwa Krajowego - PLN 76.5 billion, which represented 67.6% the share of the total amount of potential non-required liabilities at the end of 2015. At the same time, the share of commitments included in the category of commitments with an average risk of their repayment from the budget was 17.4% at the end of 2015. , and in the category of low-risk liabilities - 82,6%. None of the guarantees were classified as higher or high risk commitments and therefore the increase in potential non-required liabilities of the State Treasury was not too high and the share of medium risk liabilities remained low acceptable. The Treasury debt at the end of 2015 amounted to PLN 834.5 billion, which means that it increased by PLN 54.6 billion during the year. The main reason for the increase in this debt was the financing of the government budget deficit and the deficit of the European budget of PLN 45.8 billion and the loans from the State budget granted to the Social Insurance Institution to finance the current activities of the Social Insurance Fund of PLN 5.5 billion, as well as the loss of contributions from the FUS to open pension funds of PLN 3.1 billion. Moreover, due to exchange rate differences, the Treasury's debt increased by PLN 8.8 billion. The share of national debt in total debt was 65.1% , and foreign debt - 34,9% As of the end of 2014, these shares have changed by 0.6 percentage points in favour of national debt. The net lending needs of the state budget amounted to PLN 53.8 billion last year and were PLN 4.9 billion lower than planned. The financing of loan needs was mainly obtained through debt issuance, i.e. 35 billion PLN, of which the issue of treasury securities on the domestic market was 89.5% , and from debt to foreign markets - 10.5%. It was possible to reduce external financing due to the reduction of lending needs and the possibility of obtaining funds on the domestic market under favourable conditions. The Minister of Finance also used gold and currency funds collected on the accounts at the National Bank of Poland and Bank Gospodarstwa Krajowego to finance loan needs. The use of consolidation funds for the day-to-day management of the State budget has allowed for a reduction in the issuance of treasury securities. This solution was beneficial as the cost of raising funds from the issuance of treasury securities would be higher than the cost of raising funds as a result of consolidation. The higher level of net lending needs compared to 2014 was due to the need to finance a higher government budget deficit. The negative aspect of budget implementation was that in 2015 the level of arrears towards the state budget amounted to PLN 76,9 billion. However, it should be noted that this was partly the result of an increased frequency of detection of irregularities during tax checks, with a lower number of checks carried out. This may indicate an improvement in the selection of operators for control, but also the persistence of the large size of the grey economy and the still high risk of tax fraud. In 2015, average employment in the national economy amounted to 9789 thousand. people and was 0.9% higher compared to the previous year. Average employment in state budget units amounted to 563 000. people and was down by 2.8 thousand. people compared to average employment over the last 4 years. At the same time, last year's employment growth in the processing industry was quite strong, by 2.5%. in relation to 2014, and in trade, by 2,2%. Indeed, because by 7.6% , employment in the information and communication industry, including IT services and the activities of telecommunications companies, has increased. Employment in public administration and national defence was maintained at almost unchanged level from 2014. 1.2% Employment in the education sector has increased. The average monthly gross remuneration was 4.2% in 2015 higher than in 2014. According to the Central Statistical Office, wages in the sector of enterprises employing more than 9 persons increased by 4.5% , and salaries in the budgetary sphere - 3.4% , while the average monthly payment of the pension benefit from the Social Insurance Fund amounted to PLN 1967.16 and was 3.4% higher in relation to the previous year. The average pension financed by the pension fund of KRUS amounted to PLN 1054,50 in 2015 and was higher by PLN 44 compared to the previous year. The number of unemployed registered at employment offices at the end of 2015 amounted to around 1.6 million people and was lower by around 262 thousand. persons than at the end of 2014. The unemployment rate at the end of 2015 was 9.8% , which means that it decreased by 1.6 percentage points compared to the previous year. The long-term unemployment rate decreased to 3% in 2015 and was lower than the European Union average of 4.5%. The unemployment rate was also low among older workers aged 50 to 64, which in the fourth quarter of 2015 was the lowest in at least 10 years and was 4.9%. at the EU average 6,9. Poland coped with youth unemployment much worse. In 2015, the unemployment rate in Poland among people under the age of 25 was 20.8% , i.e. it was higher than the average in the European Union, although slightly. In the European Union, this indicator is 20.3%. In 2015 15.5% Polish residents were at risk of relative poverty compared to 18.1% Ten years earlier. This means that despite favourable economic changes in Poland, there was still a large group of households at risk of poverty. I will now turn to the committee's work directly on the 2015 budget implementation report. During the work on the 2015 budget implementation report, the Committee on Public Finances considered, in accordance with the order of the Marshal of the Sejm, the budgetary parts in its competence as well as those which were the subject of the work of other committees. Other committees have submitted 25 opinions to the Committee on Public Finance, including four opinions resulting from the joint work of the Joint Committees. All the opinions submitted were positive, although the Committee on Economic Affairs and Development expressed a negative opinion with regard to the Office for Competition and Consumer Protection, indicating that the negative opinion on the implementation of the budget in this part is due to irregularities in expenditure found during the audit carried out by the Supreme Chamber of Control for a total amount of PLN 573 900, including those in breach of law - 489 thousand. zł. These included: on the non-compliant classification of expenditure on modernization works in the building of the UOKiK included for renovation instead of property expenditure on investments, non-compliance with a contractual penalty of 16,2 thousand. PLN and expenditure of part of the funds received from the general reserve not in accordance with the purpose for which they were granted. At the same time, the Committee on Economic Affairs and Development informed the Committee on Public Finance that, in view of the findings of the checks indicating an infringement in the Office for Competition and Consumer Protection of Public Finance discipline, the Supreme Audit Office would send an appropriate notice to the competent Ombudsman of public finance discipline. The explanations made in the Committee on Public Finance indicated, inter alia, that for Part 53: the Office for Competition and Consumer Protection, the Supreme Audit Chamber issued a descriptive opinion because the criteria for a positive and negative assessment were not met. A representative of the Chamber, giving a detailed response to the irregularities found during the inspection at that office, informed that the office had raised objections to the opinion of the NIK, but the College of the House rejected these objections. The Public Finance Commission considered the report on the implementation of the State budget in 2015 at meetings held from 22 June to 6 July inclusive. During her meetings, the Committee on Public Finance took note of the opinions of other committees, the opinions of the referees, the positions of the Supreme Audit Chamber paying attention to the shortcomings and irregularities found and the studies of the Sejm Analysis Office. The members of the committee asked questions, a discussion took place, although some parts of the budget were not discussed, because they were adopted immediately after the information was submitted. The materials did not raise any objections. During committee meetings, additional explanations were provided by representatives of the ministries and other budget managers, and some budget managers were asked to submit detailed explanations in writing at a later date. In particular, this was the case, among others. effects of restructuring of mining, trade in green certificates in the context of the climate package and revenues from this and the amount of subsidies for local government units over the longer term, taking into account the number of pupils and the announced education reform. In view of the positive opinions of the committees and the members of the referees, with one exception concerning part 53, as I said earlier, the Committee on Public Finance received without objection positive opinions on the implementation of the state budget in individual parts, special funds, executive agencies and other forms of economic activity. Discussions at committee meetings and the findings of the NIK indicate that, however, there are a number of challenges for the institutions responsible for public finances that should be taken into account in their future activities. There are also a few problems that the Committee on Public Finance will address in the near future. As regards matters relating to the work of the Committee on Public Finances on the report on the implementation of the last year's budget, the High House should be informed that the President of the Supreme Audit Board informed the Committee on Public Finance, inter alia, at the last meeting of the committee on the examination of the report on the implementation of the budget on 6 July. that on 8 June the College of the Supreme Audit Chamber adopted a resolution on discharge for the Council of Ministers, expressing a positive opinion, noting that the state budget and the budget of the European funds were implemented in accordance with the revised budget law. Budget revenue was 0.8% higher in relation to the plan and expenditure reduced by 1.5%. The planned deficit of PLN 50 billion amounted to PLN 42.6 billion and the revenue generated allowed to finance around 87% expenditure of the budget. The report on the implementation of the budget shows a fair picture of the implementation of the state budget, but it was noted that the financial situation of the Social Insurance Fund further deteriorated despite a higher grant from the state budget by about PLN 11.7 billion. In 2015 there was also a deterioration in the fiscal income ratio of the public finance sector of GDP to 18.3% from 18.6% In 2014, the College of NIK pointed out the need to take further steps to seal the tax system. The House audited 109 parts of the budget and, on the basis of the assessment methodology developed, did not issue any negative assessment for the implementation of the last year's budget - it welcomed 91 parts of the budget and applied a descriptive assessment in respect of 18 parts due to failure to meet the positive and negative assessment criteria. Recent experience with budget control shows the need for more control of the units subject to the ministers. The President informed the committees of the irregularities that had occurred at the Institute for Environmental Protection, which had not previously been audited, with regard to the conclusion, implementation and clearance of civil law contracts within the framework of the commercial activities carried out by the Institute, as well as the personal decisions taken, i.e. the change of the Director of the Institute. In conclusion, the NIK's assessments for the implementation of the budget in 2015 were slightly better than for the implementation of the budget in 2014, although the conditions for the implementation of the budget were still complex. On 6 July, the committee also voted on the negative opinion of the Committee on Economic Affairs and Development with regard to Part 53: the Office for Competition and Consumer Protection and adopted a draft resolution presented to the High House. In this draft, the committee proposes, in point I, the adoption of a report on the implementation of the State budget for the period from 1 January to 31 December 2015, and in point II, the granting of discharge to the Council of Ministers for that period, and in two subsequent points, to draw attention to irregularities in the implementation of the State budget in Part 53: the Office for Competition and Consumer Protection and to actions to address other irregularities identified by the Supreme Audit Chamber. other budget managers, too. In conclusion, the committee requests the Sejm to adopt a draft resolution contained in the Seym printing no. 700. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]
ParlaMint-PL_2016-07-20-sejm-23-2.u5	Thank you very much, sir. I ask the President of the Supreme Audit Chamber of Mr Krzysztof Kwiatkowski to present an analysis of the implementation of the state budget and monetary policy assumptions in 2015 and an opinion on discharge for the Council of Ministers.
ParlaMint-PL_2016-07-20-sejm-23-2.u6	Mrs. Marshal! Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen, Minister! In fulfilling the obligation under Article. 204 of the Constitution of the Republic of Poland, I have the honour to present on behalf of the Supreme Audit Chamber an analysis of the implementation of the state budget and monetary policy objectives together with the opinion of the College of the Supreme Audit Chamber on discharge for the Council of Ministers for 2015. [[President of the Supreme Chamber of Control Krzysztof Kwiatkowski illustrates his speech with slides]] Following a review of the implementation of the State budget and an analysis of the findings made in the course of the review, the Supreme Audit Office positively assessed the implementation of the 2015 budget law. In practice, due to parliamentary elections and a change of government, the evaluation of execution is an evaluation of the work of the two governments. The Supreme Audit Chamber, with the reservations indicated in the analysis, welcomed the keeping of accounts for the implementation of the State budget and the budget of European funds and the reliability of the annual accounts. The NIK also considered that the report of the Council of Ministers on the implementation of the State budget for the period from 1 January to 31 December 2015 presents in all material respects a true picture of revenue, expenditure, receivables and liabilities. These assessments were drawn up on the basis of the results of the controls in 230 entities. Nearly 27% of these controls have been examined in detail. State budget and European budget expenditure of nearly PLN 108 billion. The reliability of the accounting records and the budgetary reporting were confirmed after checking the accounting evidence of 29% expenditure of the state budget and of the budget of European funds for a total amount of more than PLN 116 billion. At the same time, it should be emphasised that the control of expenditure made from the accounts of the principal authorising officers, which was initiated in 2015, allowed for a significant increase in the scope of the control, measured by the percentage of the budget expenditure audited. At the same time, this was done without increasing the cost of budgetary control. This is the distinction of this year's budgetary control, i.e. an analysis of implementation for the previous year. It is also worth noting that the percentage of budget expenditure audited by the NIK - which concerns both the total expenditure and the expenditure made by the third-degree managers - is significantly higher than that determined by the commonly used international auditing and financial control practice. On the basis of the findings of the audit, the implementation of the budgets in 109 budgetary parts and the implementation of the financial plans of 35 out-of-budget economy units and special-purpose funds has been assessed. In nearly 4/5 cases, the implementation of budgets and financial plans received a positive assessment, as President Jacek Sasin said. Every fifth part of the budget or the budgetary unit has been found to cause a reduction in the assessment. In these cases, the assessment was presented in a descriptive form in order to best reflect the scale and nature of the irregularity, due to the lack of methodological qualification for a positive or negative assessment. Following a study initiated in the budgetary control, in response to signals indicating significant irregularities in the conclusion of contracts with the staff of the Institute for Environmental Protection - the State Research Institute, I decided to carry out an additional ad hoc check in that unit. It resulted in a negative assessment of the regularity of the conclusion, implementation and settlement of civil law contracts in the context of the commercial activities carried out by the Institute during the period from 1 January 2013 to 20 May 2016. In this institution, NIK questioned in particular the legality of contracts with a total value of over PLN 3 million. The examination of accounts was carried out in 20 budgetary parts. In 17 of them, the reliability of the accounts was positively assessed. In two parts opinions were formulated with reservations, and in one case, i.e. in the Ministry of Justice, no accounts were kept for the authorising officer of the budget, despite the adoption of State budget appropriations of nearly PLN 4.5 billion and the transfer of them to subordinate directors. An examination of the annual accounts was carried out in 101 parts of the budget and 150 responsibilities in the third degree. NIK has given a positive opinion of more than 90% the reports examined. In the Ministry of National Defence, the presentation of the data on additional annual salary obligations, i.e. the so-called. 13th salary. This irregularity consisted in the fact that the authorising officers did not include such obligations in the accounts of the year to which they relate. The Supreme Audit Chamber also concluded that nine budget managers, contrary to the provisions of the Finance Minister's Regulation of 16 January 2014 on budgetary reporting, did not draw up reports at the end of the entitlement to share part and that the reports drawn up did not take account of the creation of new budgetary parts as of 27 November 2015. In our view, this has resulted in a lack of transparency in the implementation of the state budget in this section. The findings of the audit of irregularities in the accounts and budgetary reporting were amended or did not have a significant impact on the compliance of the information and data indicated in the report of the Council of Ministers with the implementation of the State budget for the period from 1 January to 31 December 2015 with the provisions in force in this respect. High Seym! In 2015, the state budget and the budget of European funds were implemented in a context of a moderate growth of the European and global economy. Global GDP growth remained at 3.1% 3.4% in 2014 gross domestic product in the European Union increased by 1,9% in 2015 , and in the euro area countries by 1.6%. Against this background, the economic situation of Poland developed positively. In 2015, there was higher economic growth than in 2014. Gross domestic product increased really by 3.6% While the year before the increase was 3.4%. Domestic demand remained the main driver of economic development. The economic recovery was accompanied by an improvement in the labour market situation. The recorded unemployment rate, as has already been mentioned, decreased from 11.8% In 2014 to 9,8% at the end of 2015 Export dynamics were higher than import dynamics. This resulted in an improvement in the balance of trade. In 2015, for the first time since the beginning of the 1990s, there was a surplus in foreign trade in Poland. As a result, the current balance of payments account deficit decreased from 2% GDP in 2014 to 0,2% GDP in 2015. The value of foreign exchange reserves expressed in zlotys increased from PLN 352 300 million to PLN 370 300 million, although this was also influenced by the weakening of the zloty compared to the US dollar and the UK pound. Part of the macroeconomic performance forecasts drawn up for the work on the draft budget law have proved to be inconclusive. This was particularly the case for inflation forecasts. The government assumed that the prices of consumer goods and services would increase by 1.2% in the average annual schedule. In the meantime, they dropped by 0.9%. This resulted in a decrease in revenue from the tax on goods and services estimated according to the assumptions made between PLN 800 million and PLN 2600 million. The draft budget law for 2015 was prepared in accordance with the applicable law. In order to ensure the implementation of the budget law under the conditions of expenditure discipline, remuneration limits have been maintained for most units of the public finance sector and for the managers of public positions. The so-called mechanism was also used. stabilizing the expenditure rule. High Seym! The total revenue of the state budget and the European budget amounted to PLN 353 600 million last year and was slightly higher than the revenue generated in 2014. The revenue of the State budget increased by PLN 5600 million, but it did not reach the level planned in the Budget Act of 15 January 2015 and was lower by PLN 8100 million. Tax revenues had a decisive impact on the volume of income. The revenue from the tax on goods and services was higher than forecasted in the revised Budget Act by PLN 1800 million, but lower than that achieved in the previous year by PLN 1100 million and above the forecast adopted in the Budget Act of 15 January 2015 by PLN 11 500 million. The main reasons for the non-performance of the originally forecasted VAT revenues were the overestimated projections of the tax revenue in 2014 and its deteriorating collection in 2015. On a large scale, invoices documenting fiction have been put into circulation. Those involved in this activity have reduced their tax obligations by using the lack of effective system instruments to address this phenomenon. While the tax authorities were able to detect part of the fraud in this area, it was still low in determining the actual tax fraud organisers and in enforcing the duties imposed by the tax authorities' decisions. Detailed findings on this issue were provided by the Supreme Audit Chamber in the information on the results of the anti-marketing audit of invoices documenting the fictitious activities of April this year. The effectiveness of the collection of public-law danins deteriorated in 2015. The tax and other budgetary arrears at the end of 2015 amounted to PLN 69 900 million and increased by almost 34% compared to the end of 2014. In 2015, it amounted to around 40%. , while in 2014 it was about half lower. On the other hand, revenue from income taxes was fully realised. Secondly, for 2015, the final settlement of payments resulting from the implementation of the multiannual programme of equipment of the Armed Forces for F-16 multitasking aircraft was carried out. In the budget of European funds 58% expenditure covered expenditure under the National Strategic Reference Framework and 37% - incurred in financing the common agricultural policy. From the 2004 accession to the end of 2015 Poland has received EUR 122 600 million from the European Union budget. In 2015, as in previous years, the Minister of Finance used legal solutions to demonstrate a lower government budget deficit than the economic nature of the operations carried out. These solutions were applied to the financing of the Social Insurance Fund and the purchase of F-16 multi-purpose aeroplanes. The support offered to the Social Insurance Institution consisted in the granting of loans by the State Treasury to provide payments from the Social Insurance Fund and to transfer funds from the State budget to supplement the loss of contributions transferred by the Social Insurance Institution to open pension funds. The total funding from both titles amounted to PLN 8600 million. At the end of 2015, the loan balance reached PLN 45 300 million. The probability that they will be repaid is low. In view of this, the Supreme Audit Chamber maintains the position that we have been passing on for years that all FUS funding should be included in expenditure rather than in expenses, as it is de facto non-refundable and not financed by privatisation revenues. Also part of the multiannual programme of equipment of the Armed Forces of the Republic of Poland with multi-purpose aircraft was financed from uses, not expenditure of the state budget, because for 1 day a loan was taken to finance this payment. The Supreme Audit Chamber does not dispute the legality of the financing agreements concluded, but notes that the acquisition of aircraft from an economic point of view constituted an investment expense which was not fully disclosed in the budget records. The result of financing the acquisition of assets from the government budget was the demonstration of a lower deficit of the state budget by PLN 1800 million. In summary, if these transfers were eligible for expenditure according to their economic nature, the government budget deficit would be higher by PLN 10 500 million and would amount to PLN 53 billion, including the deficit of the European budget would be PLN 56 300 million. According to the NIK, it is advisable to eliminate, reduce legal solutions which, in our view, ensure less transparency in the public finance economy. The loan needs of the state. Net lending needs amounted to 2015 53 800 million PLN and were lower than planned by 4900 million PLN. The biggest contribution to the development of loan needs was the deficit of the state budget and the funding allocated to the Social Insurance Institution. The funding for the financing of loan needs was mainly obtained through the issuance of debt. National funding was dominated in 2015. It was possible to reduce external financing due to the reduction of lending needs and the possibility of obtaining funds on the domestic market under favourable conditions. We notice this change, and we think it is a positive trend. The Minister of Finance provided full and timely handling of payments of the State budget. In particular, there was a transfer or withdrawal from privatisation contracts due to the investments planned by the companies, the unregulated legal status of the company's real estate or the reprivatisation claims addressed to the companies or simply the lack of interest of investors in the privatisation. The financial plans of the audited out-of-budget economy units have in most cases been properly implemented. However, three important issues relating to State-owned special-purpose funds need to be underlined. Firstly, the financial situation of the Social Insurance Fund is steadily deteriorating. Its condition fell by PLN 5600 million in 2015, despite obtaining a grant from the state budget of PLN 42 billion. The increasing imbalance between the revenue and expenditure of the Fund poses a risk to the state's finances in the longer term. Secondly, some special-purpose funds have maintained significant cash surpluses, transferred to the finance minister's management. Already in previous years, the House indicated that these measures should primarily serve to finance the tasks for which the funds have been set up. The retention on accounts of a high level of resources shall not contribute to the achievement of the objectives set out in the laws. Total revenue of funds. The remaining 25 realized only 2,3% the revenues of this group of entities. The Supreme Audit Chamber stresses that a large number of non-budget public finance units and fragmentation of the financial economy increase the costs of public administration and do not promote effective control of public finance discipline. This relationship was lower than that achieved in 2014 by 0.6 percentage points, it is worth noting. Poland has fulfilled the criterion obliging the Member States of the European Union to maintain the general government deficit below 3% GDP. The improvement in the financial situation of the State gave rise to the completion in June 2015 of the excessive deficit procedure for Poland, which has been ongoing since 2009. Further decline in tax revenues in relation to GDP should be considered a worrying phenomenon. Further erosion of tax revenues may, in our view, threaten the proper functioning of the State in the long term, especially since the gradual decline in tax revenues in relation to GDP was not due to the implementation of a specific fiscal policy of the State, namely tax reduction. The average value of this relationship in the European Union at the end of 2015 was 72.5% and decreased by 1 percentage point compared to 2014. The majority of Western European countries have a higher debt-to-GDP ratio than in Poland, including Germany, the United Kingdom and France. The scale and seriousness of the irregularities identified by NIK as a result of the State budget implementation checks, as I said, were not significant enough to reduce the assessment of the implementation of the state budget as a whole. The irregularities found most often consisted of: failure to investigate or unsuccessfully recover budgetary claims, including tax duties, errors in grant awards and insufficient supervision of their use, failure to comply with the provisions of the Public Procurement Law, misconstruing of property expenditure, exceeding the authorisation to make expenditure, keeping accounts in a manner that is not in line with the provisions of the Accounting Act, errors in accounting records in budgetary statements, errors in carrying out construction investments, insufficient supervision, including the implementation of multiannual programmes. I am, of course, talking about these errors in the context of the number of budget units that we have audited, but I would stress once again that the scale of these irregularities is absolutely non-excessive for the overall assessment that I presented at the beginning. In addition, in the course of the changes in the organisation of the public finance sector in November 2015, in the form of the creation of new branches of government administration and the amendment of the budgetary components, the ministers affected by the amendments concluded agreements on the disposal of budgetary parts without a legal basis. As a result, despite the establishment of new budget components, the tasks assigned to them were carried out by other contributors. Such agreements arose before the end of the financial year. The results of the audit show that it is necessary to continue the efforts aimed at: developing and successfully implementing the tax sealing strategy, carrying out a thorough analysis of the functioning of the Finance Minister's bodies and developing solutions to ensure effective enforcement of the taxes due and to improve the effectiveness of these bodies, ensuring the financial stability of the social security sector, strengthening the supervision of part-holders of the implementation of financial plans, in particular in the area of debt recovery, the use of grants and the implementation of investment intentions, and improving the forecasting of budgetary revenues. The NIK also notes the need to finalise the evaluation report and the conclusions of the report on the impact of the performance of the public finance commitment and to carry out a consistent review of public expenditure in 2015 and to monitor the implementation of the recommendations resulting from them. Where the level of expenditure is determined by a stabilising expenditure rule, the expenditure reviews should be an important instrument for shaping the most desirable structure of public expenditure. High Seym! In fulfilling its constitutional obligation, the Supreme Audit Chamber also carried out an audit of the activities of the National Bank of Poland in implementing monetary policy objectives in 2015. In 2015, the Council 3rd year in a row inflation developed below the monetary policy objective set out in monetary policy. It was to keep inflation at 2.5% with an acceptable deviation interval of ±1 percentage point. However, from January to December, there was a deflation. The increase in prices of consumer goods and services ranged from -16%. to -0.5%. Deflation has been determined by the decline in raw materials and food prices, i.e. factors outside the sphere of monetary policy impact. In this context, the Monetary Policy Council considered that, since there was no significant disturbance in the economy, there was no stronger response than a one-off reduction of 0.5 percentage points. There was no disturbance in the economy, as economic participants considered deflation to be a temporary phenomenon. It therefore did not have a significant impact on their decisions. However, the persistence of price decreases can change this attitude and increase the risk of deflation turning into a phenomenon with negative consequences for the economy. In such a situation, the cumulative adverse effects of this phenomenon on tax revenues would increase. As a result of the audit, the Supreme Audit Chamber did not find irregularities in the NBP's application of monetary policy instruments. Mr. Marshal! High Seym! I will start by saying that financial risks are an important element in the security of public finances, state finances. Guarantees and guarantees have an impact on the way this financial risk develops, therefore Article. 216 of the Constitution tells us that loans and guarantees must not exceed, in short, 60% GDP. Today's GDP is 1789 billion, so this limit should not exceed about PLN 1060 billion, according to the Constitution. Therefore, the control of guarantees and guarantees is important, as I said, for the security of the state's finances. Therefore, this limit, although decreasing from 300 to 200, is disproportionately high. For the time being, the financial situation is quite stable, so it is used to a small extent, but the Budgetary Act secures this limit of as much as 200 billion, at least in 2015, and in 2014 up to 300 billion, so that there are appropriate tools and measures to protect the financial security and stability of institutions that may possibly benefit from these guarantees and guarantees. ParlaMint-PL_2016-07-20-sejm-23-2.u9	Thank you very much, sir. I now ask the President of the National Bank of Poland, Mr Adam Glapiński, to present a report on the activities of the National Bank of Poland in 2015.
ParlaMint-PL_2016-07-20-sejm-23-2.u10	Good morning. [[Good morning.]] Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! As President of the National Bank of Poland and Chairman of the Monetary Policy Council, I have the honour to submit an annual report on the activities of the National Bank of Poland to the Sejm of the Republic of Poland, in this case from 2015. By virtue of the article. The Commission shall adopt implementing acts laying down detailed rules for the implementation of this Regulation in accordance with the examination procedure referred to in paragraph 1 of this Article. 1 The Constitution of the Republic of Poland has the right to determine and implement monetary policy. As the central bank of the Republic of Poland, NBP is responsible for the value of Polish money. Article 4 is replaced by the following: 3 of the Act on the National Bank of Poland clarifies the powers set out in the Constitution, stating: "The basic purpose of NBP's activities is to maintain a stable level of prices while supporting the economic policy of the Government, unless this limits the basic objective of NBP. The National Bank of Poland strives to maintain price stability by implementing the inflation target strategy, which is based on anchoring long-term inflation expectations at the level of the announced inflation target. Since 2004 NBP pursues its medium-term inflation target of 2,5% with a symmetrical deviation interval of plus minus 1 percentage point. During this period, the average dynamics of consumer prices in Poland amounted to 2.1%. At the same time, the central bank shall take into account the lessons learned from the global financial crisis. For this reason, the NBP's monetary policy is being pursued to promote sustainable growth and the stability of the financial sector. In this way, NBP supports the government's economic policy and seeks to maintain price stability over the long term. However, this monetary policy requires a flexible approach to the inflation target, which mainly involves accepting the periodic deviation of price dynamics from the target. This flexibility is particularly needed when there are unexpected shocks in the global economy, strongly affecting national price processes. In 2015, the main shock in the global economy was the sharp decline in raw material prices on global markets. Oil prices fell by 46% in 2015 compared to the previous year and reached the lowest level since 2004 at the end of 2015. The strong price decrease concerned most of the most important raw materials, including agricultural raw materials. As a result, global consumer price dynamics have fallen to the lowest level in half a century. The exception was 2009 when its decline was linked to the global financial crisis. As a result, inflation in most countries was significantly lower than their inflation targets in 2015. Recordlessly low price dynamics in the world have been conducive not only to a strong fall in raw material prices but also to a moderate increase in demand in the global economy. Under such conditions, the main central banks continued their expansionary monetary policy, although there was a discrepancy in the activities of the European Central Bank and the US Federal Reserve. In 2015, for the first time in many years, the Fed has increased its interest rates, while the ECB has significantly increased its monetary expansion. In the European Union, there has been a gradual, although slow improvement in economic activity. At the same time, the economic situation in emerging economies, including the most important of these economies, i.e. China, has clearly deteriorated. Eastern trading partners of Poland, i.e. in Russia and Ukraine, a permanent recession. The economic downturn in developing countries, while the start of interest rate increases by the Federal Reserve, has led to a deterioration in the global financial markets. As a result, capital was drained from emerging economies and the prices of financial assets in these countries decreased significantly. In these circumstances, many emerging economies' currencies have also been clearly weakened, including. The golden course. In Poland, as in many European countries, there has been a decrease in the overall level of consumer prices, which was minus 0.9% in 2015. Deflation was mainly due to the strong impact of the global decline in raw materials prices on domestic energy and food prices and low inflation in the Polish economy. At the same time, the economic situation in Poland was good and the decline in raw materials prices had a positive impact on the economic situation of most economic operators. Deflation didn't work. the negative impact on economic activity. Inflationary expectations were well anchored and households treated deflation as a transitional phenomenon. Therefore, no weakening of retail dynamics and a sharp increase in the propensity to save has been observed. The decrease in raw material prices also contributed to improving the profitability of companies despite the systematic increase in nominal wages. This was already mentioned by the President of the Supreme Audit Chamber, who appeared before me. When conducting monetary policy last year, the Monetary Policy Council took into account the fact that the persistence of deflation was the result of external factors, as indicated in the NIK report, independent of national monetary policy and had no negative macroeconomic impact. In particular, the Council considered that deflation was accompanied by an acceleration of GDP growth of 3.6% in 2015. There has also been an increase in consumer demand, which has contributed to a clear improvement in the labour market situation and good domestic moods. There has been a strong increase in employment and the unemployment rate has fallen to record low levels. This was accompanied by a stable increase in wages, the real value of which was further increased as a result of the negative dynamics of consumer prices. As a result, the assessment of the financial situation of households has improved. At the same time, the good financial and liquidity situation of the companies was maintained. In particular, the percentage of viable companies was high. Export has also increased strongly in 2015 and its dynamics have been highest for 4 years. In view of such circumstances, the National Bank of Poland softened monetary policy in 2015 and 5 March 2015 lowered interest rates to the lowest level in history. The NBP reference rate was reduced by 0,5 percentage points to 1,5. The reduction in interest rates was intended to reduce the risk of price dynamics being sustained below the inflation target. When deciding on the scale of the reduction of interest rates in 2015, the Monetary Policy Council took into account the external nature of the sources of deflation and the absence of negative effects on the economy, as well as the prosperity of the country, including stable GDP growth and a clear improvement in the labour market. In its decisions, the Council also took into account the systematic growth of national lending, the dynamics of which have been among the highest in the European Union in recent years. In this way, monetary policy reduced the risks to the stability of the financial system, which could result in excessive reduction of interest rates in good times. In monetary policy, the Monetary Policy Council has therefore taken appropriate action to increase price dynamics over the medium term. The scale of monetary policy mitigation was also adapted to the continuing economic prosperity, thus reducing the risk of macroeconomic imbalances in the Polish economy. Mrs. Marshal! Mr. Messengers! Gentlemen! The monetary policy of the National Bank of Poland in 2015 was conducted under conditions of continued overliquidity of the banking sector, although its size during the year showed a downward trend. The average level of overliquidity of the banking sector in 2015 amounted to nearly 90 billion PLN and was by more than 17% lower than a year ago. The NBP's monetary policy was implemented through a set of instruments adopted by the Monetary Policy Council. Unlike many foreign central banks, the situation in Poland did not require NBP to use unconventional monetary policy instruments and allowed for a conservative approach to monetary policy. The set of instruments was therefore the same as in the previous year. The main monetary policy instrument in 2015 was the NBP interest rates. The NBP reference rate was the most important factor in the implementation of monetary policy. The changes in its amount were the direction of the NBP's monetary policy. This rate, determining the profitability of open market operations, affected the interest rates of short-term money market instruments. The NBP's deposit and pawns rate set a range of interest rate fluctuations in in-bank transactions. The main open market operations were the main tools used to manage the liquidity of the banking sector. They consisted of regular weekly issuance of cash vouchers by NBP with a maturity of 7 days. In determining the value of the issue, NBP sought to maintain balanced liquidity conditions in the banking sector. In the event of a significant liquidity imbalance, the NBP could also use fine-tuning open market operations. In 2015, such operations were used to a small extent. They were only in the form of issuances of NBP cash vouchers with a maturity of less than 7 days. In 2015, the operational objective of monetary policy was to develop the Polonia rate at a level similar to the NBP reference rate. The average absolute deviation from the NBP reference rate in 2015 was 12 basis points and was similar to that achieved in the previous year. Compared to the crisis in the financial markets, the deviation of the Polonia rate from the NBP reference rate decreased approximately seven times. This demonstrates the high effectiveness of NBP in achieving the operational objective of monetary policy in 2015. Mrs. Marshal! Mr. Messengers! Gentlemen! The statutory task of the National Bank of Poland is to shape the conditions necessary for the development of the banking sector and carry out activities for the stability of the national financial system. Since 1 November 2015, an action to eliminate or reduce systemic risk has been added to these tasks. Maintaining the stability of the financial system and countering the rise of risks in it are essential conditions for achieving the constitutional goal of NBP, which is to ensure the value of Polish money. Before the recent crisis in the world economy, central banks focused mainly on maintaining price stability. However, the experience of the crisis has shown that they cannot limit their mandate only to conduct monetary policy and maintain inflation at a low level. At present, central banks are also expected to promote the stability of the financial system and ensure the balance of the economy as a whole. The financial system plays a key role in the transmission of monetary policy impulses to the real sphere, and its destabilisation may make it difficult or even impossible to operate effectively. As early as 2011 the NBP initiated actions to create macro-prudential supervision in Poland, drawing up, in consultation with the Ministry of Finance, the foundation of a draft law. He called for, among other things. the establishment of a body responsible for the conduct of macro-prudential policies. Finally, on 1 November 2015, the Act on macro-prudential supervision of the financial system and crisis management in the financial system entered into force. The Act has designated the Financial Stability Committee as the competent authority for this supervision. He is now responsible for both the development of macro-prudential policies and the coordination of crisis management activities. The main objective of the Committee in the framework of macro-prudential supervision is to strengthen the resilience of the financial system in the event of systemic risk materialisation and thereby promote long-term sustainable economic growth. The work on macro-prudential supervision is chaired by the President of the National Bank of Poland. The National Bank of Poland provides organizational and substantive support to the committee through the development of analyses of the sources of systemic risk and the preparation of proposals to limit it. In the area of crisis management, the Committee is still chaired by the Minister of Finance, and the Ministry of Finance provides services in this area. The first meeting of the Financial Stability Committee on macro-prudential issues was held on 23 December 2015. Among other things, it was discussed. issues relating to different sources of risk to the financial system relating to the functioning of financial institutions at low interest rates, the costs of restructuring and bankruptcy of credit institutions and the bank tax. The Committee informed the institutions of the European Union, i.e. The European Council on The systemic risk, the European Banking Authority and the European Commission, of decisions taken on macro-prudential policy. Effective measures for financial stability require an understanding of the current situation of the financial system and the identification and assessment of the risks at stake. The National Bank of Poland carries out this task by conducting its own research and analysis of the financial sector, as well as cooperating with other state bodies in the creation of legal regulations and taking actions that promote the stability of this sector. The NBP's analysis confirms that last year the Polish financial system was stable, and the most important segment of it, the banking sector, was in good condition, although its net financial result decreased by as much as 18.9%. - from PLN 15.9 billion at the end of 2014 to PLN 12.9 billion at the end of 2015. In the sector as a whole, the rate of lending continued to be close to the nominal rate of GDP growth and its capital facilities were adequate to the level of business. The capital ratio at the end of 2015 was 16% to the 12% required by supervision. However, the profitability of the banking sector has decreased, which has been influenced by both low interest rates and certain developments in the banking sector. The banks incurred the costs of providing the Creditor Support Fund for which they paid PLN 600 million. They also incurred additional fees for the Bank Guarantee Fund, PLN 2.04 billion, in connection with the announcement of the bankruptcy of SK Bank and the need to pay guaranteed deposits to its customers. My previous speakers talked more about this. They also had to pay higher regular contributions to the BFG - Bank Guarantee Fund than in previous years. The total amount of all these burdens together reduced the net profit of the sector by around 14%. At this point, it is worth paying more attention to the above mentioned bankruptcy of SK Bank. On 20 August 2015, at the request of the Management Board, the NBP granted him a refinancing loan of PLN 500 million, of which PLN 450 million was used. The guarantee granted to the loan was the State Treasury guarantee covering half of the amount paid and, in the remainder, the transfer of SK Bank's claims. The NBP's decisions on granting liquidity support were taken on the basis of Article. 42 Act on the National Bank of Poland. It allows the central bank to grant banks a refinancing loan. The central bank acted as the lender of the last instance, as the Management Board of SK Bank no longer had any market opportunities to obtain funding. The support provided by NBP enabled SK Bank to carry out its current accounts and was intended to allow the preparation of a resolution programme or to seek the buyer of the bank to take over and restructure it. In its decision to grant a refinancing loan, the NBP was guided by the opinion of the Financial Supervision Authority on the solvency of SK Bank and took into account that it established a committee board to prepare a resolution programme. The results of the audit of the Bank's financial statements published on 12 November 2015 by the statutory auditor indicated the bank's highly negative own funds due to the need to create special provisions and to verify the valuation of collateral. As a result of the correction made, the assets of SK Bank were not sufficient to meet its liabilities. As a result, on 21 November 2015, the Council adopted Decision (CFSP) 2015/1333 [2] KNF suspended SK Bank's activities and applied to the court for bankruptcy and finally announced the bankruptcy of SK Bank on 30 December 2015. SK Bank's bankruptcy costs were borne by all banks and its uninsured creditors. The payment of deposits guaranteed by the BFG amounted to over PLN 2 billion. These funds have been paid from the Guarantee Fund. The funds of this fund are collected by all banks in the form of Treasury securities or NBP cash vouchers. They should therefore have been cashed in and paid in the corresponding amounts to the BFG. This was a direct cost for banks and had a negative impact on their financial performance for the fourth quarter of 2015. Last year's crisis of SK Bank, where the most expensive solution, i.e. bankruptcy, and other events of minor importance, were necessary justify the need to introduce into the Polish legal order a body responsible for the compulsory restructuring and orderly liquidation of banks and SKOKs, which has an appropriate instrumentarium. The National Bank of Poland has repeatedly pointed out the need to implement the Resolution Directive into the Polish legal order. We are therefore very pleased to note that in June this year. The Sejm passed a bill in this regard, and the president signed it. As a result, the new regulations will be in force in the autumn. The solutions provided for in the Act will provide financial security networks with a wider and cheaper set of instruments to reduce the public costs of solving the problems of financial institutions. Mrs. Marshal! Mr. Messengers! Gentlemen! An important element of the activities of the National Bank of Poland is the management of foreign exchange reserves. Under the conditions of the liquid exchange rate and the conduct of monetary policy in line with the strategy of the direct inflation target, foreign exchange reserves aim primarily at strengthening the financial credibility of the country. Their size is analysed by foreign investors, international institutions and credit rating agencies. In this way, the value of reserves held affects the cost of financing on global markets and the volatility of the gold exchange rate, as well as reduces the risk of rapid outflow of capital. Incidentally foreign exchange reserves may be used under conditions of significant disruption to the functioning of the financial system, in particular to counteract excessive fluctuations in the exchange rate. The National Bank of Poland aims to ensure a stable increase in foreign exchange reserves, allowing to maintain an appropriate level of commonly used reserve adequacy indicators, reflecting in particular the potential need for short-term financing in times of crisis. The main source of reserves is the purchase of foreign currency related to the inflow of funds from EU funds to Poland. Official reserve assets increased by EUR 4.2 billion in 2015. This increase was mainly due to changes in the foreign exchange relationship of reserve currencies. It also contributed to the income from the investment of reserves and the positive balance of external flows. When managing foreign exchange reserves, the National Bank of Poland strives to ensure a high level of security of the funds invested and their proper liquidity. When these criteria are met, the NBP shall take action to increase the profitability of the reserves. However, it depends on market conditions, exchange rate changes, prices of investment instruments and the level of profitability. In 2015, the Council The NBP reached a rate of return calculated in PLN of 5,5%. The appreciation of the US dollar and the pound of sterling against the gold contributed the most to this. The income of the reserves expressed in the currency of the instruments was only 0.9% and was limited by the low yield of government bonds, mainly linked to the expansionary monetary policy of the main central banks, and by halting the upward trend in long-term debt securities prices. The US dollar and the euro dominate our monetary reserve structure. NBP also invests in British, Norwegian, Australian and New Zealand securities that offer higher profitability. The official reserve assets of the NBP are also included in the monetary gold stock of approximately 103 tons. However, the attractiveness of the gold reserves is limited due to the high volatility of its price, as well as small investment opportunities. In 2015, the income of NBP for investments in gold amounted to PLN 6.9 million, or PLN 28.7 million. High Izbo! One of the bank's statutory activities is the issue activity. Pursuant to the Act on NBP, the central bank has the exclusive right to issue cash marks to the Republic of Poland. At the end of 2015, the value of cash in circulation amounted to PLN 163.2 billion, and compared to the previous year increased by PLN 20.3 billion, i.e. by 14.2%. The high increase in cash in circulation observed for the past 2 years is mainly due to economic growth and the consequent increased demand for cash from businesses and households. For several years now, the central bank has been working systematically to optimise money circulation in Poland. These include, in particular, reducing the cost of issuing banknotes and coins, representing around 1/3 of the operating costs of the NBP. As a result of the joint work of the Ministry of Finance and the NBP in January 2015, the IMF Executive Board granted Poland access to a flexible credit line for the next 2 years. In January 2016, after the review carried out by the MMA of Poland's compliance with the criteria for access to the line, the value of the available funds at the request of the Polish authorities was again reduced and currently amounts to around 17,9 billion U.S. dollars. The National Bank of Poland supports the line as a financial agent of the government. Access to a flexible credit line is an additional security for our country in the event of a sudden deterioration of the external situation. Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! This institutional arrangement facilitates the coordination of both types of supervision and enables a more efficient exercise of the central bank's stability functions. International experience shows that the location of micro-prudential supervision at the central bank enhances the effectiveness of the supervision itself and the institutions of the financial security network, and is particularly necessary in the event of tension or crisis. For this reason, this solution is implemented in most European Union countries as well as in the Banking Union.
