ParlaMint-PL_2016-07-20-sejm-23-2.u14	Yes, as the rapporteur of the committee, I will try to present the course of discussions at the meeting of the finance committee on the implementation of monetary policy in 2015 and the activities of the National Bank of Poland, together with the analysis of the Supreme Audit Chamber, which took place on 22 June. The overarching objective of monetary policy is to maintain stable prices and to maintain the conditions for economic growth, to ensure macroeconomic balance, internal balance, and to stabilise the financial system of the State. These objectives, which have been implemented for years, were also treated in the case of the action of the National Bank of Poland, which was responsible for monetary policy, in 2015. The nature of monetary policy, i.e. whether it is an expansion policy, a low interest rate policy or a restrictive policy - we can approximately describe it as a high interest rate policy - is affected by the situation in the economy, the external situation and the internal macroeconomic situation. The price decrease of world energy raw materials, as well as the significant price decrease of agricultural raw materials, has been a source of price depression in Poland as well. Despite the improvement in the euro area, economic activity was still low. The situation in emerging economies has deteriorated. The pace of economic growth in China was dropping, and some countries were actually hit by recession. In addition, we have the threat of a new wave of financial crisis in the countries of the European Union and the unpredictable effects of Brexit. These factors affected the nature of monetary policy implemented by the National Bank of Poland. The lack of wage growth in Poland and despite the improvement of the high unemployment rate, close to two figures, did not create sufficient demand and thus sufficient price pressure to offset the price depression caused by the above mentioned reasons. As highlighted here, inflation was -0.9% in 2015 So we've been dealing with the phenomenon of deflation. Deflation reduced debt handling costs - this is a positive impact - but it also reduced revenue to the state budget by about PLN 2 billion. The Monetary Policy Council considered that the Polish economy was approaching or operating close to the limit of generation capacity, so that, in fear of exceeding this limit of generation capacity, it maintained additional rates, did not apply negative rates. As mentioned, although there was a deviation from the inflation target of 2,5% , on average a year up to 3.4% , the largest in comparison to all countries, the Monetary Policy Council in March reduced the interest rate by 0.5 percentage points to 1.5%. This demonstrates the great caution of the Monetary Policy Council, which, with the highest deviation from the inflation target in February, to the level - 4.1 the percentage point, reduced the interest rate by 0,5 percentage points. In this respect, There could be doubts as to whether there was a discussion or whether such a cautious policy was really justified here. The existing huge reserves of resources, even if human capital in the form of unused and available labour in our market, as well as other resources, other reserves in this area, would seem to allow, with security and with the help of these monetary policy instruments, greater activation of the economy. This is, of course, a matter for discussion. The central bank, using these classical instruments, such as the change in reference rate, foreign exchange reserves and open market operations, influenced the supply of money in circulation and thus the volume of global demand, the macroeconomic equilibrium in Poland, thus strongly affected the economic activity of all operators - producers, investors and consumers, although they maintain a high degree of independence under market economy conditions. Thus, the Polish central bank, the National Bank of Poland plays a huge role in shaping dynamics, production, employment, exports, has a strong impact on the economic situation. At its meeting on 22 June, the Committee on Public Finance, considering the report on the activities of the National Bank of Poland in 2015, drew attention to several issues, including: to the fact that budget support, in accordance with the opinion of the Supreme Audit Chamber, was carried out properly and in a timely manner. The National Bank of Poland launched the electronic banking system and led over 14 thousand. bills for more than 3,000. entities in the public finance sector. The National Bank of Poland achieved, as mentioned, a positive financial result exceeding PLN 8 billion, although it was planned at 0% , which has benefited from the revenue of the State budget in connection with the transfer of this profit to the State budget. Achievement of such a high profit was the result of a positive result on exchange rate differences and a positive result on foreign reserve management. The National Bank of Poland achieved a profit from foreign exchange reserves management, which we also appreciate positively, because the state of foreign reserves, and especially their relation to debt, is an indication of security in the assessment of investors and credibility of Poland in the eyes of investors. The level of assets of the National Bank of Poland also increased last year by 5% and reached 86, almost 87 billion euro. In terms of gold, it increased by 5% and reached EUR 370 billion. It should also be noted that the rate of return on foreign reserves remained high, especially taking into account the impact of the exchange rate changes, i.e. the rate of return calculated in PLN remained at 5.5%. If the effect of exchange rate changes is not taken into account, this indicator was 0.9% , we are talking about the rate of return, i.e. both in one case and in the other case we have a clear deterioration compared to 2014 of the rate of return of both meters. This is the result of the international situation, the profitability of government papers and many other processes that took place both in the euro area and outside of it due to the recovery in the United States, where the US dollar's appreciation and the pound of sterling took place as a result of the change, which led to a slightly lower rate of return than in previous years. Returning to the activities of the National Bank of Poland, it should be emphasized that the National Bank of Poland worked intensively with the Ministry of Finance on the issue of public debt management, issued securities, organised tenders in the context of open market operations on buying-in, as well as the sale of treasury papers. The reserve for exchange rate risk amounts to over PLN 11 billion, including last year it was increased by PLN 2467 million. This protects the financial result against the appreciation of the gold. For several years the Polish banking system has been characterised by overliquidity. It is estimated at the level, as the President mentioned, about 90 billion PLN. Although it is decreasing, that the Polish banking system and the National Bank of Poland are getting better and better, this is the case. This is mainly due to the buying-in of currencies in connection with the sale, the servicing of debt, the conversion of the Member State contribution, as well as the Union funds. In the implementation of the second very important function of the National Bank of Poland, which is to ensure the stability of the financial system, the National Bank of Poland cooperated with the Ministry of Finance, the Financial Supervision Commission and the Bank Guarantee Fund. This cooperation was also initiated within the Financial Stability Committee, although such a meeting took place only once, it was created in August ub. Some findings, including information on the security of the banking and financial system in Poland, which is required to be communicated to various EU organisations, have been prepared and communicated by this committee. In 2015, the Council The NBP granted a refinancing loan to restore liquidity to one of the banks, which unfortunately left the National Bank of Poland with an unpaid 150 million-dollar loan - not paid by the bank due to its bankruptcy. Here, the attention of the members of the committee was drawn to a certain fact and this was the question raised as to whether the granting of this loan was preceded by a sufficiently thorough analysis. However, after the dispute with the syndicate ended, it turned out that the National Bank of Poland would be able to claim claims from collateral in the form of a guarantee of the Treasury, not from the mass of bankruptcy, which is beneficial for the national bank. It was also noted that this situation resulted in a lack of resolution procedure, which, previously implemented - the deadline for its implementation was 2014 - would avoid such negative consequences for the National Bank of Poland and the entire banking sector. In the bank's own economy, the Supreme Audit Chamber did not find any irregularities, although it was stressed that such an audit of NIK takes place every few years, every four to five years, and is fragmented, i.e. it concerns an analysis of some expenditure only, which may lead to a risk of finding irregularities in a comprehensive analysis. The withdrawal of the National Bank of Poland from the payment of the contribution of 2,600 thousand should be positively assessed. zł to the European Banking Authority, since this would be without a legal basis. This will increase the revenue to the state budget, and contrary to the concerns of some members of the committee, this will not translate into the elimination of the National Bank of Poland from participation in the work of the European Banking Authority, because this contribution will be paid by the Financial Supervision Commission or the appropriate legal basis will be prepared. ParlaMint-PL_2016-07-20-sejm-23-2.u104	Mrs. Marshal! High Seym! In answer to your questions, which have not yet been answered, I may start with a question that has crossed over several times in the speeches, both in the presentation of the position on the report on the implementation of the state budget and in the questions of individual Members. This concerns the questions of Mr Sitarski, Mr Meysztowicz, as well as the statements of Mrs Hennig-Kloski, who pointed out that the loan, as far as the FUS is concerned, was de facto leading to a deficit of about PLN 8 billion and, if added, a 3% deficit in the public finance sector would also be exceeded. I would just like to draw attention to the fact that in 2015 this additional loan to the Social Insurance Fund to fulfil the State's obligations to pensioners, i.e. the State's guarantees regarding the payment of these benefits, was transferred in the amount of PLN 5.5 billion rather than PLN 8 billion. In any event, regardless of its size, it did not translate into additional State debt in any way because, regardless of whether it would have to be issued in order to transfer subsidies to the FSO, or whether it would have to issue papers and obtain funds for the granting of the loan, the same expenditure from the Social Insurance Fund was covered in any case. And all expenditure from the Social Insurance Fund was taken into account when counting the public finance sector and absolutely did not exceed these 3%. As we know, this deficit has reached 2.6%. The questions raised by your Members are also the question of why this loan was given. According to the Social Insurance Fund Act itself, the sources of funding for this fund have several options. This is both a grant and a loan as a full mechanism for balancing the Social Insurance Fund. Of course, at this moment, when we have different rules, already resulting from the Public Finance Act, the counting of public expenditure, where we have a stabilising expenditure rule, this division into a loan and deficit is already losing out on as important as it was 2 and 3 years ago. Of course, as you will notice on the basis of concrete data, statistics on the amount of these loans, they gradually fall every year. The natural consequence will be here - we hope that as early as possible, perhaps next year - the departure from this loan system, although the possibility of lending from the state budget itself in the budget itself, because there are provisions on this subject, should remain, because it gives some flexibility. If, as a result of a situation that could not be foreseen at the planning stage, the calculation in the Social Insurance Fund plan, or other circumstances which would result, for example, in the planning phase. more pensioners or pensioners that are not foreseen at the planning stage... Such an optional loan mechanism should always exist in order to ensure that the State's obligation towards pensioners and pensioners to provide these payments can be fulfilled. Today, however, as I said, the fact that it is a loan does not translate in any way into debt or the outcome of the public finance sector, and it was in line with the Public Finance Act itself. Mrs Skowrońska asked about the draft law on the income of local self-government units - this project has already been addressed to the High House - in which it extends the functioning of the corrective and equalisation mechanism which is still in force in 2016 - a new correction and equalisation mechanism which is addressed exclusively to voivodships. Is the Podkarpackie Voivodeship - that was the question of the Member - going to lose 100 million PLN on this movement? As in 2015, and in 2016 the solutions adopted by the government last week in the law amending the law on the income of local government units will in no case result in any voivodship, including the Podkarpackie voivodship, losing any resources. These solutions are designed, planned and included in this project, on which the High Chamber will work, in such a way that the sum of funds transferred to voivodships despite the change of the system would be the same as if the system had been in force in 2014, i.e. before the time when the Constitutional Tribunal questioned the solutions at that time on application and the complaint lodged by the Mazowieckie voivodship at that time. So, in conclusion, I would like to say that certainly the Podkarpackie region will not lose any resources on this solution - of course I am talking about the denomination - compared to those that would have been obtained under the old system. Mr Kasprzak asked about the fall in unemployment and linked this question to the increase in expenditure on active forms of combating unemployment in 2015. How much did the expenses increase? In the 2015 Labour Fund plan, expenditure on active forms of combating unemployment was to increase by PLN 582 million with the end. I'm rounding up these amounts here so they can have some better reception. Of course, this was the plan, but if we take into account the implementation of these active forms of combating unemployment, this increase in funds spent in relation to 2014 amounted to PLN 244 million. Of course, the fact that the amount is less than the plan is due to the possibility of distribution, demand, compliance by the addressees of the laws which can benefit from these measures, but certainly no longer from the limitation of this plan, but to the various natural phenomena which caused the potential beneficiaries of these measures not to reach or meet the conditions for obtaining them. Mr Sitarski, in addition to the questions concerning the Social Insurance Fund, which I have already answered, also asked how the government intends to reduce the share of foreign debt. The course of action to reduce this foreign debt is set out in the debt management strategy, and this strategy results in a successful reduction in this share of foreign debt, which will mainly involve a higher issuance of securities on the domestic market and, of course, a lower debt issuance in foreign currencies. Of course, it is difficult here to assign specific influences to specific actions, because the sum of these influences is taken into account. In six months' time, as has already been said by Minister Shamacha in recent days, there has been a clear increase in income, including tax revenue. Of course, it is difficult to talk about all the revenues here, because we know that in 2016 there were also one-off revenues associated with the sale of frequencies. Of course, I'm leaving it on the side. But the same tax revenue also increased year by year for the same periods for each practically a few percent, so this increase in this income is noticeable. Stand by. It is now about 18 billion dollars. These charges, which are related to the maintenance of this limited flexible credit line, are about PLN 188 million in the state budget. What are the benefits? The benefits are linked to maintaining the security and sustainability of public finances, with the possibility for us to maintain this flexible credit line, to fund the state's finances, whether in terms of securing loan needs and not being able to obtain these funds otherwise in the event of various economic or geopolitical turmoil, or in accordance with the principles and opportunities offered by the programme of this flexible credit line, with the possibility to cover and support balance of payments. ParlaMint-PL_2016-07-20-sejm-23-2.u118	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! On behalf of the Polish Free and Solidarni Circle, I present my position on the Government's draft law on the amendment of the Personal Income Tax Act and the Corporate Income Tax Act. In the current legal situation, both large companies, with undoubtedly wider access to finance their development and investment, and small enterprises, meeting the criteria of a small taxpayer, which have limited access, are equally subject to tax. The reduction of CIT for small business activities carried out by corporate income tax taxpayers and for the newly created activities during the first year of their activities, despite the negative impact on the state budget, will ultimately be a step in the right direction towards accelerating the growth of entrepreneurship in Poland, which should offset the lost individual influence. The provisions introduced in the procedinated amendment limit the possibility of benefiting from a reduced CIT tax rate for taxable persons starting their business as a result of the restructuring of the company. The preferential 15% corporate income tax rate is only obtained by such taxpayers, starting in the third year of the tax. Such a solution will only counteract restructuring in order to benefit from reduced CIT. The remaining regulations introduced by the Act are intended to seal the tax system. Precise changes to the current rules will eliminate the doubts that arise when they are interpreted, which translate into tax avoidance of certain income. In addition, by restricting the application of certain tax preferences, transactions aimed only at obtaining tax benefits and amending the rules used by taxpayers in the optimisation schemes will not only increase revenue to the state budget but at the same time affect the level playing field of entrepreneurs on the market. Thanks to the tax rules, as indicated by the Ministry of Finance, the reduction of CIT for small taxpayers will not affect the level of revenue to the state budget. The bill is therefore neutral in this area. Now we are waiting for a proposal for a law to increase the tax-free amount. The Free and Solidarni Circle will vote in favour of further proceedings of this law. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

This is indeed due to the fact that this provision of the detailed clause is implemented through the introduction into Polish legislation of the Tax Directive applicable in the case of restructuring. Certain provisions are being introduced on a certain preference, which were implemented many years ago - the 2009 Directive - and the special tax regime for restructuring - and the application of a specific clause which makes it not used as a tax gap. We want as part of the fact that in practice we have seen some application and such incomplete, you can say, implementation, with some holes... That is why we are taking this opportunity to clarify these matters. As far as Mrs Tokarska is concerned, I have the impression that the wording that entrepreneurs themselves will pay for tax reduction is a little bit to increase because we have to pay attention to what is in this law. In this law there is above all a tax reduction for small companies. Here I answer Mr Gosievski's question immediately. These companies are 390 thousand, for the total number of 420 thousand. entities which are taxpayers of CIT, i.e. 390 000. These are those who will pay less tax and the remaining 30,000. These are those for which the tax will remain at the current level, 19%. Actually, it's 390,000. this is more than 90% of all CITs, i.e. the tax reduction affects more than 90% companies, of course companies paying tax in the form of CIT. Indeed, this document, this legal act, and especially the OSR, shows that tax revenue reduced by tax reductions is offset by the fact that tax revenue from those who have tried to use different tax optimization methods will be increased. These methods in this legal act proposed by us are at least five groups. It is therefore difficult to justify that they are the same entrepreneurs. The aim of this project is to reduce taxation for small businesses by implementing the intention and announced plans of Prime Minister Beata Szydło, but on the other hand, it is intended to ensure that taxes are actually paid by those large companies that have specialised consulting companies, legal firms, which try to pay as little taxes as possible, using all legal gaps. And with this legal act, we're trying to seal the legal system. I have the impression that this is completely justified so that this plan is actually implemented. At the same time, I answer Mr Sitarski's question, what is it like with this share fetch? In fact, this is the element of the sealing work. We are talking about a certain detail of the provisions that aim to ensure that non-residents, i.e. persons who have non-Polish citizenship, who benefit from certain activities in the financial market in Poland, actually start paying taxes on income that they achieve in Poland. And in that sense, This is one of the elements that shows how much this project is needed. It is simply that those who earn money in Poland must pay tax on income. In fact, it is not what you said it was a new tax imposed on individuals in Poland. This element concerns non-residents who so far avoided taxation, made money on the Polish capital market and in fact, using legal gaps, they simply did not pay tax in Poland. About the question... It's all in general. Full agreement that what is the goal, what is most important, is that entrepreneurs pay less taxes in Poland. And you could say... I understand the need of the High House to simply lower taxes in Poland. This is actually incredibly important. It's a little bit like this bill is a stage in the whole process. This is the first stage to reduce tax for small companies within the CIT framework, but we have in the background really a tax change, which will most likely start in 2018 under the so-called. a uniform tax on which work is carried out in the Ministry of Finance in KPRM. Minister Kowalczyk spoke about this. There is a project to ensure that this single tribute, this uniform tax, is set up to reduce the taxation of labour income for the least earners, while at the same time changing the way of taxation of business, so that the non-tax part actually, because unrelated to such direct competences of the Minister of Finance, i.e. social security contributions, is not too difficult for start-ups or developing companies to ensure that entrepreneurship in Poland is not restricted. For this reason, it can be said that this is the first step: a uniform tax, a facilitation for business. An overview of the rates related to tax contributions to the Social Security Office in the case of economic activities, it can be said that this is the second stage that is planned in the future. Actually, it's a work in progress. In fact, it will only start functioning from 2018, mainly due to IT problems related to the fact that this is a big project consisting of the fact that a single contribution will most likely be charged simply by one entity. And the final findings. The issue of Mrs Michalek's question about CIT receipts in the last 4 years and this year. Unfortunately, I do not have this information on me, and I therefore propose a written reply. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

The problem I will be discussing is the investment in health care. So far, investments in our country have often been insufficiently thought out. This was often more due to the ambitions of individual decision-makers than to actual regional needs. This led to situations such as the creation of two hospitals in one place, close to each other, which competed with each other, using only part of their capabilities. The Commission, when allocating EU funds, is guided by priorities such as transparency, rationality. In order to obtain funds, she demanded - which was initially questioned - health maps and priorities of regional health policy. These documents are created... because they're not all created yet. However, it has been challenged that the epidemiological, demographic, infrastructure-related data collected there do not translate into specific investment decisions. Such a tool to enable such decisions to be taken is the instrument for assessing investment proposals in the health sector - the name in short is IOWISZ. A system of relevant questions was created under the auspices of the Minister and was created by a team of experts. Here the explanation was that now different competitions are stopped, which can just be started only... i.e. On 1 July, competitions have already been launched, but in order to be able to make evaluations, there must be tools, such as sets of questions and surveys. Another question was the marshals - why there are no marshals in the decision-making circles. The answer was that the marshals are the founding bodies of hospitals and could be biased here. The doubts were detailed, the Minister can certainly bring them closer in a more extensive way, and they have been explained. The amendments tabled and voted on were legislative, possibly editorial. They were accepted. ParlaMint-PL_2016-07-20-sejm-23-2.u130	Mr. Marshal! Minister! There have been numerous indications that there is no link between the putting into service of the investment and the contract with the National Health Fund. The simultaneous ban on the provision of paid benefits increased the non-use of the funds involved. Such a policy has led to absurd situations, when in a given region, e.g. Several dozens of thousands of residents were formed by a number of one-name branches, which were de facto empty. According to NIK's audit, the current legal situation does not guarantee a rational investment policy in health care. In the current state of law, there is no indication of the public authority responsible for coordinating the activities of all actors involved in the application process, inter alia. for funds from the European Union. The Law and Justice Club welcomes the draft law and expresses its thanks to the Ministry's staff for the rapid and efficient preparation of the IOWISZ IT tool for the objective assessment of investment proposals in health care, hoping that the European Union's health funds will be used rationally and contribute to improving the quality and availability of the benefits provided. ParlaMint-PL_2016-07-20-sejm-23-2.u134	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! These opinions need to respond in real terms to existing health needs. Therefore, they are to be issued by voivodships taking into account maps of health needs, regional health policy priorities and data from the register of healthcare providers. In July 2015, it was announced by the then government, and required by the European Commission, a document called policy paper. It points out that having a positive opinion of the voivode or the Minister for Health will have an impact on the assessment of the provider's offer. The lack of a positive opinion will result in fewer points being awarded. By all means, an entity not having a positive opinion will have to present a much better offer than the offers of those who have received a positive opinion from the voivodship. This will ensure that the new system does not block market access for new entrants and, on the other hand, will ensure reasonable investment in areas where there is a balance between demand and supply for medical services. The Minister of Health will be able to define criteria for selecting offers that will ensure the right choice of the best providers. In a word, after the proposed provisions have entered into force, a positive opinion on the purpose of the investment will be influenced by the provider to conclude an agreement with the National Health Fund. In the case of own funds investments, the opinion obtained will also provide an important indication of the appropriateness of the measures taken. It is hoped that this will prevent the irrational development of the medical services market. Positive opinions are to be issued only if actual resource shortages can be identified on the basis of health needs maps. With the introduction of the law, it is expected that there will be fewer unthinkable and unthinkable investments for the health system. The introduced system will allow real use of health maps and priorities for regional health policy. As a result, it will enable the sustainable development of the health system and counter the waste of resources. In view of the above, the Poselski Kukiz Club15 will support the proposed bill. Thank you.

ParlaMint-PL_2016-07-20-sejm-23-2.u152	Dear Marshal, Ladies and gentlemen! Before I go to the specific questions, I would like to provide an overview of the work on this law and on the tool of IOWISZ, the Investment Proposal Assessment Instrument in the Health Service. Well, ladies and gentlemen, I have adopted a draft map of health needs, in which there are actually 12 billion zlotys of EU funds, a large part of which is devoted to investment in infrastructure. Ladies and gentlemen, the main problem that this instrument, this law, is the fact that the first maps of health needs that have been published - and I answer the Member's question - were published last December. These were maps of cardiology and oncology. On the other hand, maps on closed care, hospitalization, were published on 30 April. Of course, there was a risk that the European Commission would not take these maps away from us. These documents, maps of health needs, as you know, include information on epidemiology, demography, infrastructure and human resources. These are very extensive documents. They contain 1000, 1200, up to 1500 pages per voivodship. According to legal requirements, further ranges of maps for specialized care and primary health care will need to be created by the end of December this year. The good news is that we have succeeded, and the European Commission has received from us these maps that have been published. They've been picked up. Therefore, it can be said that the project started several years ago is implemented in a timely and effective manner. On the other hand, ladies and gentlemen, was there a risk that the European Commission might not have received the maps and that the project could have been subject, say, to doubt whether it could have been subject to any criticism? Of course, this risk was, ladies and gentlemen, mainly because maps of health needs, these extensive collections of various types of data do not directly translate into investment plans, into investment decisions. Also, ladies and gentlemen, legally required and developed on 30 June so-called. the priorities of regional health policy, i.e. nothing more than strategic plans for regional change, within different areas, also, please countries, do not translate directly into investment plans, into decisions on the implementation of individual infrastructure investments. This was a serious problem, ladies and gentlemen, because the European Commission requires us first of all to improve the transparency of the spending of funds. Secondly, so that we can improve the reasonableness of the funds spent. I mean, not to build two hospitals side by side with the same profile that compete with each other for the same money from the National Health Fund. If the National Health Fund has, let's assume, 20 million PLN in a given area and so far it has been enough for the operation of one hospital and next to the establishment of a second hospital, and the NFZ will divide these funds into these two hospitals, they will both be in financial difficulties. It was also about not buying expensive diagnostic and therapeutic equipment, which is then unpacked, and we are already applying for further purchases. Ladies and gentlemen, it was necessary to meet the requirements of the European Commission, and the Minister for Health has therefore set up a team to translate the information contained in the health needs maps and in the priorities of regional health policy into investment decisions, as it was designed two years earlier. It was then that the duty of the voivods to issue opinions about the purpose of the voivodship came into force. Ladies and gentlemen, we do not change here those legal regulations and processes that have already been established and legally confirmed. Ladies and gentlemen, the essence was that the decisions about purpose were to be made. So we set up a team. When it comes to maps of health needs, we briefly call these people, these experts cartographers. Cartographers are few specialists who are in Poland, who are familiar with indicators. This is actually a very difficult play. In order to develop appropriate criteria, appropriate indicators, you need to have knowledge from several very different areas. Cartographers worked for many months, until the end of June practically, as a matter of urgency. Why this urgent procedure, there was a question here. Twice, that's a question. Well, ladies and gentlemen, according to the Act from 30 June, you can apply to the voivodships for opinions on the purpose of the investment. So these proposals are already being made. We had to develop an urgent tool, this tool, this IOWISZ, is actually a decision support tool. It does not replace the decision and the whole process, which ends with the opinion of the voivode of purpose, but strongly supports this decision. I mean, it makes the whole process more transparent and rational. For what reason? Therefore, I would ask you that the questions that are in this instrument, in IOWISZ, relate to a number of elements that the European Commission was concerned about. I mean, they obviously needed transparency, rationality, but also greater emphasis on long-term care, palliative care, environmental care, this closer to the patient. However, thanks to these criteria, we use these questions to fill in the whole process of processing applications for certain investments in order to meet the health policy objectives. But there are also questions which concern the inclusion of a given investment in the implementation of the Polish economy's development policy. There are criteria, you'll see, 31.33, which act very strongly and according to the proposed formula can help in the development of the Polish economy. As someone mentioned here, the resort takes the view that 12 billion PLN in this project maps of health needs is a lot of money. But that money can, of course, hurt us if they're wrongly spent. If they are well spent, they can of course help both the health service and the Polish economy. But badly spent can be a kiss of death for us. As far as the NFZ question is concerned, there is indeed a deep reform of the system currently being prepared, where the NFZ will also disappear as a payer. Ladies and gentlemen, in this bill we refer to the state of play. Wherever the NFZ disappears and a new paying authority appears, there will automatically be all these changes. Therefore, it seems to me that this corresponds fully to your request. As I say, opinions about purposefulness, the fact that the voivode gives these opinions, was introduced by law two years ago. However, ladies and gentlemen, this law ensures this transparency and rationality not only because these criteria or these questions are well thought out and fit into the three elements I mentioned earlier. In addition, it is assumed that the voivode gives an opinion on the purpose of the investment, but first the applicant must complete the relevant score. Therefore, he can check whether he fits in with these objectives of health policy and development policy of the Polish economy. Then, of course, the National Health Fund, which has additional information, can also look at these answers and may comment on this. Then the voivode and her back room can change their previous opinions. Of course, this will always be done with justification. With regard to the answer to your next questions, of course, the opinion of the voivode is preliminary and some procedures then include, at the next stage, the decision of the steering committee, while some will be subject to the competition procedure. However, I would like to remind you that the opinion of the voivode, although preliminary, is a formal, statutory requirement introduced 2 years ago. Ladies and gentlemen, also the policy paper, which is currently not negotiable, provides for an opinion on the purpose of the investment. This change is also impossible at the moment, as is the fact that the financial resources of this project widely called maps of health needs are already allocated to particular regions or voivodships, and we cannot change that either. This is the state we found after the previous government. This money, it seems... it's impossible to renegotiate this division. This tool, on the other hand, is designed to divide funds that are already allocated to voivodship, within the voivodship, so that it is rational, transparent, fits into health policy and development policy and meets the requirements of the European Commission. Ladies and gentlemen, IOWISH is a de facto barrier to entry. It means that if an investment is irrational, then I WOULD JUST STOP such an investment, or it would have to be modified by the originator so that it would meet the goals I had previously discussed. However, IOWISH, ladies and gentlemen, has actually another, in my opinion, much more important function. In addition to the fact that we have prepared this tool in the form of such a tool for the regulation, a computer program is also prepared, it will be published on the website. There anyone who has an idea will be able to see what score this idea gets, whether it fits into those goals I mentioned earlier. Therefore, I think that very many people, very many actors, will also be able to develop such ideas to use these EU funds to help both the health service and the Polish economy. The next, ladies and gentlemen, specific questions, these first two questions from Members of the Law and Justice, namely whether they actually meet the requirements of transparency, the reasonableness of these investments. Ladies and gentlemen, this tool, as I say, does not replace the decision itself. It is a tool supporting decision-making, so that they are as transparent and rational as possible, but Summa summarum finally gives an opinion about purpose, and then there is a steering committee and competitions. However, with this tool, we will definitely be able to improve transparency and rationality. Has there been irrational behavior before? Yeah, that's what the NIK reports and a lot of other documents show. The question is when there will be plans for comprehensive changes to the health system, especially in the regions. Of course, the Voivodeships have prepared their priorities for regional health policy, they fit in, they are consumed by the tool IOWISZ, as well as the map information. Many questions must be answered on the basis of the information contained in the maps. On the other hand, if the investment does not fall within the priorities of regional health policy, the criterion is the strongest. If the investment does not fit into these regional priorities, it will not be complex at all. Of course, soon there will also be a plan for comprehensive changes in the financing of health care in Poland. I think the resort will keep you informed of this, in any case, intensive work is underway at the moment. Next, yes, indeed, trauma centers and SORs are released, because they are included in the voivodship plans, and these in turn are excluded from the maps according to the legal state already established by us. Could other highly specialised procedures be released? No, we don't anticipate that. All other investments, in accordance with the provisions and thresholds in this draft law, will apply. Are the investments started under IOWISZ? Yes, they are if someone wants to finish them using EU funds. If not, it can continue, but it seems to me that one way or another it is worth to face IOWISZ and enter a given investment, even if it is an investment in progress, because it will show what is the rationalness and systemic sense of this investment. I think it'll make sure even the investors themselves. However, I repeat once again: it is better not to use this money, the 12 billion, than to use it badly, so as to harm the Polish health service and the economy. Therefore, if the investment is made and it does not fit into IOWISH, I would suggest either a change [[Applause]] the nature of this investment, or even the cessation of it, because if it is to harm us, to harm the health service, it is better that it is not completed. That's the department's position. Come on, ladies and gentlemen. Why so fast paced, I already answered, when the maps, I already answered. ParlaMint-PL_2016-07-20-sejm-23-2.u156	Mr. Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! For me it is a great honour to present to the High House a report by the Infrastructure Committee on the Senate draft law on amending the Public Collective Transport Act. On the basis of the resolution of the Senate of the Republic of Poland of 7 July 2016 on the submission to the Sejm of the Republic of Poland of a draft law amending the Act on collective transport pursuant to Article. Article 118 (1) (a) and (b) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 1 The Constitution of the Republic of Poland of 2 April 1997. The Senate introduces to the Sejm of the Republic of Poland a draft law amending the Act on public collective transport. This law is the Act of 16 December 2010 on public collective transport. The amendment is to postpone the deadline for implementing the above-mentioned regulation. Act of one year. Six-year vacatio legis of some of its provisions did not produce the expected results. Organizers of public collective transport, i.e. local government units and passenger transport operators raise doubts as to the feasibility of fulfilling the tasks imposed on local government units to organise public collective transport. In addition, the entry into force on 1 January 2017 of certain provisions will result in passengers losing access to reduced tickets due to the fact that only 261 local authorities have chosen 349 public transport operators by 30 June 2016 and 20 local authorities have chosen 21 public transport operators by rail, while the other local authorities have not complied with the new requirements of the public public transport organisation imposed by the Act of 16 December 2010 on public collective transport consisting of inter alia. the choice of the public transport operator. Commercial carriers who do not receive compensation for the application of statutory concessions will cease to offer them. Nevertheless, congratulations should be given to the local authorities who have carried out this task. While it is feared that the introduction of amendments to the functioning of the Act, the extension of its implementation by one year, could lead to further delays by local government units in carrying out their task, it must be made clear that leaving the old deadline will bring chaos, so simply confusion and difficulties in transporting people. This will be particularly so-called. Polish county-dominant, where social exclusion in the form of deprivation of contact with workplaces or other forms of participation in public life will increase significantly. Therefore, in order to avoid various difficulties, e.g. in order to limit the possibility of carrying out reduced journeys, the draft law provides for the time limit for the entry into force of those provisions to 1 January 2018. During the committee's work, taking into account the amendments tabled by the Legislative Office of the Sejm, which were written using another editorial technique, it was possible to develop a draft presented to you in print No 725. The Commission recommends that the High House adopt this proposal. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-PL_2016-07-20-sejm-23-2.u158	Mr. Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! I have the honour on behalf of the club Law and Justice to present an opinion on the Senate bill amending the law on public collective transport. The draft law aims to postpone the date of entry into force of certain provisions of the Act of 16 December 2010 on public collective transport by one year. In addition, it amends the transitional provisions by consistently extending or rescinding the relevant rules. The Public Collective Transport Act is important for the accessibility of the country as a whole and affects the sustainable development of rural areas in particular. It should provide an efficient public transport system. Despite the six-year period of vacathio legis of certain provisions of the Act, both public transport organisers and carriers raise doubts about the possibility of fulfilling the tasks imposed for the organisation of public collective transport. The entry into force on 1 January 2017 of certain provisions will put around 15 million passengers at risk of losing access to reduced tickets due to the fact that only 9% for road transport by 30 June 2016 Local authorities have chosen public transport operators for collective transport and only 20 local authorities for rail transport. As a consequence, commercial carriers who do not receive compensation for the application of statutory concessions will no longer offer them. This may be due to the fact that the new rules on public collective transport organisations do not guarantee the creation of a system corresponding to social expectations. Among the shortcomings of the current solution, local authorities report that it is not possible to grant a public transport operator exclusive rights. The current principle of unlimited protection of competition in public transport, namely economic liberalisation, is equivalent to the deterioration of the situation of small townspeople. The essence of the exclusive right is the possibility of compensating for losses generated by the deficit line, the maintenance of which is important from a social point of view, from income from a viable line. As a result, local authorities are not interested in implementing this system. This clearly leads to a reduction in the number of deficit lines and the number of courses carried out and thus to a deterioration of the mobility of Poles. The entry into force of certain provisions of the Act on 1 January 2017 will therefore create confusion and difficulties in the passenger transport market. In order to avoid these difficulties, the current draft law provides for the time limit for the entry into force of those provisions to 1 January 2018, at the same time amending the transitional provisions. The proposed amendments will extend the period of application of the current rules on the carriage of persons in domestic transport, including the transfer of subsidies to carriers for lost revenues due to the application of the reductions. Members of the Parliamentary Club The law and justice will support the proposal submitted. Thank you. [[Applause]]

According to information provided by the Association of Voivodships of the Republic of Poland and the Marshal's Offices, a large group of local government units has not yet complied with the requirements of the public collective transport organisation imposed by the Act of 16 December 2010. This will also allow local authorities to organise public transport in their area in order to best meet the needs of their residents. This will also allow for an in-depth analysis of the target shape of public collective transport organisations at this time and possible legislative changes in this area. Taking into account the views of external actors involved in the proceedings for the drafting of this bill, it should be noted that there is a need to adopt legal solutions providing for mechanisms for cooperation between powiats and municipalities which will enable the creation of an integrated public collective transport system. For these reasons, the delay in the date of entry into force of the Act will allow the extended period of vacathio legis to be used for the necessary amendments. It is necessary to adopt a comprehensive target legislative solution in the field of public collective transport. ParlaMint-PL_2016-07-20-sejm-23-2.u188	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! Thank you so much for all those questions. This is, indeed, a senate project, but the government has a duty to address all the questions raised and to put forward a position on which the High Chamber is working. Most of the questions that have been asked concerned the government's intentions in relation to the final shape of the Public Collective Transport Act. Today we are discussing the extension of the vacathio legis, which is the matter of translating the entry into force of part of the Act, which is primarily about the method of calculating compensation for reduced journeys. At both yesterday's committee meeting and today's debate here in the plenary room, we see the compatibility of virtually all clubs, almost all of them, with regard to the need to extend the vacathio legis in this area. Therefore, I will no longer refer to this matter, because I understand that there is full compliance of the House here, and I will no longer comment on it. On the other hand, many of the questions that both yesterday and today have been raised concern about what is next about the problem, what is next. Thus, High Izbo, the government together with carriers, but also with other environments, has been working for several months on a major change of this law. This big change is necessary, even in the face of today's questions, it concerns... The law must change because the present form of it, if it entered into force in such a form, I already ignore this case, which we are discussing today, could have a very negative effect on the communication cohesion of Poland. I will therefore present to the High House a few of the fundamental principles that we will prepare and work on. I hope that as early as September, or at least in the second half of it, we will be able to present to the High House a proposal for a fundamental amendment of this law, and thus... At this point I reply that it will be sufficient time for the end of 2017, earlier, in 2017 to be clear rules for the new operation of collective transport, so that local authorities can prepare for this and carry out all necessary activities next year. We are leaving, as it is true, one year, because theoretically it would be possible to extend the vacathio legis by the end of 2018, but nevertheless we want 2018 to be a year of testing, whether these solutions that we will introduce will simply work out. There's still time to make a final adjustment so we don't make a big mistake here. High Izbo! Let me therefore make a few assumptions which will be the foundation of the project being prepared. The main proposals concern the following changes: changes in the nature of public services. The proposed amendment provides that public transport services of general interest will be able to be created by public collective transport organisers only on those lines which will not be operated by commercial carriers on a market basis. To provide commercial carriers with the possibility to apply for State subsidies for the recognition of statutory concessions, it will allow access to tickets with a statutory concession for passengers both in public transport and in commercial transport. According to the law that is in force today, only subsidies would be possible from 1 January, compensation for the relief would only be possible for carriers of general interest. The third point is to eliminate the limitation of the possibility of departing passengers only at the stops specified in the timetable. Ladies and gentlemen, it seems to me that this is not a systemic issue, but it is very distressing on a daily basis. Often there are situations where there is a statutory ban on the release of passengers, getting out of designated stops, which are on the timetable, even though these are also suitable stops. So we allow this possibility for the comfort of passengers, but we are only talking about getting off. We're not talking about getting in, we're talking about getting out. The fourth point is the differentiation of the bus fare due to the size of the bus divided into its passenger space capacity. So you can say that when you charge for the use of stops by carriers who have bigger buses, local authorities will be able to set higher prices, because this is a very precise way to say. These prices will not be harmonised, so it is an attempt to level the playing field between those with large buses and those with small ones. In the case of parts of the line, their profitability can actually be achieved only if they are operated by small vehicles, and therefore this is a very logical request that we support. The fifth point is the introduction of the obligation to design new journeys, courses in the middle of the time interval between journeys, courses already existing. Ladies and gentlemen, this is a very important problem, often in the transport market there is a conflict, new carriers reporting to operate a given line propose that the departures of their buses be 2 or 3 minutes after the already existing scheduled departures. This creates huge conflicts between carriers, but also confusion among passengers. This solution may not be ideal, because it is difficult to find the ideal solution here at all, but we want to propose it and discuss it and then possibly introduce it. The sixth point is to amend the provisions on the possibility for organisers to revoke the declaration of carriage authorising commercial operations. The proposed amendment introduces a provision that confirmation of the declaration of carriage may be withdrawn by the organiser after written warning and re-recognition of the same infringements. This point, as the representatives of the carriers have reported, is needed because in the current form of a law, which is very strict in this respect, in fact even a minor breach can cause the possibility of the operation to be revoked. We do not want such strict solutions, we understand that all of this needs to be resolved rationally, and we need to be resolved in such a way as to serve customers first and foremost, but also with respect for the interests of carriers. Next, the exemption of buses carrying out regional services of persons on lines up to 50 km from tolls on sections of tolled national roads. This request was made by Minister Andrzej Adamczyk during his previous term of office, today we enter him into the bill and we will proceed over it. Finally, ladies and gentlemen, there are a few very important issues that have emerged from today's discussion and have also appeared in the questions. First of all, it is a matter of agreeing and coordinating transport plans. This is a case that is very neglected today. Of course, today there is no obligation of such coordination, it is only a kind of recommendation, but also an unanswered one in the law. It is therefore necessary to consider that the mechanism for coordinating and agreeing transport plans between different local authorities is simply effective. This is necessary because it is of great importance for communication cohesion, for the comfort of passengers, for the possibility of using transport. Another case that didn't sound like it today, but I'd like to refer to it as well. Namely, we must also refer to rail transport during the work on this major amendment. Because he can play - and plays - a very important role and should play an even more important role than he currently does. We also see that even in the plans of local governments, in these transport plans, this rail transport was very poorly considered. Of course, the vast majority of local governments do not have access to the railways, just as in Poland is, such is the poor railway infrastructure. However, this should be taken into account. High Izbo! I would also like to address the voice of Mr Maciejewski, chairman of the committee of government. It is indeed a very good idea that together - we are open as a ministry for debate in this area - that when determining the final shape of the bill we should be in constant contact and in constant debate, and in constant consultation with representatives of the local government, because they are primarily implementing and responsible for this transport today. We want to develop such a solution which will serve all participants in this market, so important for maintaining the communication coherence of Poland. And, ladies and gentlemen, yesterday during the discussion in the committee, proposals were made for possible amendments to the High House to be considered. We in the Ministry have carried out a very thorough analysis of these amendments tabled at least orally at the time. As regards the 1st amendment, which concerns the extension of the validity of authorisations ending between 2016 and 2017, we have reviewed this thoroughly, and it is that this law solves this problem and there is no need to take this additional amendment into account. Finally, two other amendments. One of these concerns the refusal to extend the validity of authorisations - this matter will actually be regulated in this large law. 3. The amendment concerns the issue of confirmations of transport declarations and this issue will also be regulated in a major amendment. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2016-07-20-sejm-23-2.u190	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! The Committee on Infrastructure, on the other hand, after first reading and considering the draft law at its meeting on 19 July, asks the Sejm to adopt the same draft law. Ladies and gentlemen, the project consists of only two articles. Art. 1 of the project provides for the addition of a new article in the airport specialization, art. 4a. It concerns a new deadline for setting up an airport, managing an airport or the Polish Air Navigation Agency to submit an application to the competent voivodeship for a decision to authorise an investment in an airport of public use. The deadline in the light of the existing rules was 31 December 2015, and according to the draft proposal it will be possible to submit a proposal from the date of entry into force of the Act until 31 December 2020, so we move this deadline by 5 years. According to Art. 2 draft law will enter into force 14 days after the announcement. I would add that the effects of the Act should, of course, be positive, as the objective of the airport specialization is to increase airport capacity, which is undoubtedly affecting the development of the competitiveness of our economy and the prospects for tourism development. This will certainly have a positive impact on local labour markets and regional development. Ladies and gentlemen, the plans for further investments in airports, such as the construction and extension or reconstruction of airports, can be implemented under the Operational Programme of the Infrastructure and Environment 2014-2020, specifically under the thematic objective 7. Finally, I would like to point out that there are no legislative objections to this short bill. The Act does not have any financial implications for the State or for local authorities. It does not provide for the adoption of implementing acts, nor is it subject to the regulation of European Union legislation. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2016-07-20-sejm-23-2.u192	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! On behalf of the Law and Justice Club, I have the honour to present a position on the draft law amending the Law on specific rules for the preparation and implementation of investments in the field of public use airports. The proposed proposal for an amendment to the so-called the airport specialty provides for an extension of the deadline by which the airport is established, managing the airport or the Polish Air Navigation Agency may submit an application for a decision to authorise an investment in the field of public service airport. In the current Law of 12 February 2009 on specific rules for the preparation and implementation of investments in airports of public use, that deadline was set at 31 December 2015. The proposed amendment will extend the deadline until 31 December 2020. I would like to remind you that this law sets out the rules for the preparation and implementation of investments in airports of public use. In particular, it is the construction, reconstruction or expansion of airports or facilities and facilities for operating air traffic. The biggest challenges currently in the context of the development of air transport in our country are increasing air transport capacity, maintaining air traffic control safety and ensuring safety and the highest standard of airport passenger service. To this end, it is necessary, and will, to implement a number of projects which will be eligible for EU funding. The extension of the term of validity of the specialty will facilitate the decision-making process by the relevant voivodships and facilitate the efficient implementation of the investment. The draft law will certainly have a positive impact on the efficient use of European funds and can also have a positive impact on the competitiveness of the economy and on the development of the labour market. Efficient transport and reduced travel time are those aspects that investors are paying close attention to looking for attractive places for their projects. It should also be noted that safe and efficient management of the sky will affect not only aircraft taking off and landing at our airports, because Polish airspace is an important area of transit flights. Thanks to the necessary investments, the Polish Air Navigation Agency will be able to handle safely and efficiently more aircraft flying over our country. The Law of 12 February 2009 signed by President Lech Kaczyński enabled the Euro 2012 challenge to be met. That's, among other things. thanks to this law, airports such as Modlin, Lublin were created, thanks to it modernized the aviation infrastructure both around the air and enabling the Polish Air Navigation Agency to fulfil its statutory tasks. Although the Euro 2012 has long been behind us, the new airports are developing rapidly, serving an increasing number of passengers, creating new jobs in the regions where they were created. The regulations contained in the Act allowed the Polish Air Navigation Agency to purchase land for the construction of a backup air traffic control centre, which will meet the highest safety standards. All over the world, we can continue to see the dynamic development of air transport. Poland is currently the sixth largest airspace in Europe, the sixth largest, but still chasing the top of Europe. In order to develop in order to be an equal partner, we must make every effort to ensure that aviation infrastructure continues to be efficiently upgraded and expanded. The extension of this law will enable this objective to be achieved. With regard to the transport development strategy for 2020 adopted by the Council of Ministers on 22 January 2013, it will be possible to implement the next ones set up by the above mentioned. investment strategy, i.e. increasing the capacity of existing airports' infrastructure, ensuring the conditions for efficient development of aviation and increasing the share of air transport by linking with road and rail transport. In view of the above, it is entirely appropriate to allow the setting-up of airports, airport managers, as well as the Polish Air Navigation Agency to use the solutions provided for in the specialty also after 31 December 2015 for airspace in Poland. I am convinced that the amendment of this law is in the interests of the whole country and that each club will support these positive changes. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

Thus, this document contains data on the location and area covered by the status of special economic zones, the effects of these zones, including the number of permits granted and the resulting investment expenditures, as well as the size of employment, the country of origin of capital, the structure of the sector's investment and public aid granted in special economic zones, and companies managing special zones, including. the ownership structure of the companies, their financial results and the investment expenditure incurred for their development. Currently in Poland we have 14 economic zones located in 173 cities and 248 municipalities, and the area of special economic zones occupies an area of almost 20,000. ha. In 2015, the Council of Ministers changed the borders of 11 zones, and several extensions were still under review, but the relevant decisions were taken by the Council of Ministers already in 2016. At the end of last year, entrepreneurs had 2177 important business permits, the cumulative value of the invested capital amounted to almost PLN 112 billion and the number of jobs created exceeded 312 thousand. Last year, as the Minister has already mentioned, fewer permits were issued than in 2014, these permits were 218. It is worth noting that of all entrepreneurs operating in special economic zones more than 50% are companies with Polish capital. It is often said that this aid is directed mainly to companies representing foreign capital. From this information it is clear that the situation is the opposite. The automotive industry is definitely dominated by the industry. It accounts for almost 27% of all investments. Of the 13 companies managing special economic zones, two zones are managed by the Industrial Development Agency - i.e. the Mielec and Tarnobrzeg zone - only in one Pomeranian Regional Development Agency - the pole zone - the state has neither the majority of the shares nor the majority of the votes. In other companies, the majority of votes at the general government holds the Treasury represented by the Minister of Development, although in six of them it does not have at least 50% shares or shares. The financial results of the companies last year were generally quite good. Two companies reported a negative result, he was related to the investments being carried out or transferred, and it is about adding to the costs of even depreciation. In general, companies managing economic zones have a very good financial situation. Many companies have benefited from European funds in recent years, and this is evident in investment expenditures borne by companies. They often carried out investments, among others. technology parks, which provide completely new opportunities for these actors to operate in the future. ParlaMint-PL_2016-07-20-sejm-23-2.u221	Thank you very much, sir. I invite Mrs Marta Golbik, the Modern Club.
ParlaMint-PL_2016-07-20-sejm-23-2.u222	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! On behalf of the Poselski Club I would like to present a position on the activities of special economic zones on the basis of the information submitted on the implementation of the Special Economic Zones Act as at 31 December 2015. As we know very well, special economic zones are a compilation of the needs of investors and the needs of individual regions. They are intended to enable economic activities to be carried out on preferential terms through the possibility of State aid, as well as income tax exemptions for the cost of new investment or job creation or property tax. In economic terms, the zones are part of the economic policy of the country supporting the various regions as well as the economy of the whole country. The main objective of setting up special economic zones was to accelerate economic development, to increase investment rates, to manage industrial assets and infrastructure, and to create new jobs. These Truistic statements, I believe, are, however, a starting point, and it is precisely through the prism of these assumptions that the efficiency of the functioning of special economic zones in Poland should be determined. Today we have the task of assessing the activities of special economic zones not only from the perspective of more than 20 years of their existence, but also - and above all - on the basis of the previous year, especially taking into account important changes in the provisions of the Act on Special Economic Zones implemented in 2014 and from the beginning of 2015. It is worth mentioning and analysing some of them, in particular from the point of view of their impact on the functioning of zones. The new regulations have introduced, for example, the need to submit public aid applications, enabling entrepreneurs to initiate a procedure for withdrawing and establishing the expiry of the authorisation or to regulate the procedures for the recovery of public aid granted to zones. Most of the new regulations were a logical consequence of the need to adapt legal solutions to current EU requirements and standards. However, the most significant change since last year was the modification of the State Aid Map, i.e. the maximum intensity of aid granted to zones, resulting in an average reduction of 15 percentage points for most regions. Only the so-called voivodship was excluded from this change. east wall, where the aid continued to remain at 50% , due to the need to support zones in these regions, so that they can compete with well-functioning and developing zones in other parts of Poland, e.g. with Katowice Special Economic Zone. There is no doubt that changes in the granting of State aid have affected economic zones. Indeed, the number of permits granted to investors in 2015 decreased by half compared to 2014, i.e. from 439 to 219. However, such a huge decrease is mainly a consequence of the above mentioned changes. The 2014 record figures result from the willingness of entrepreneurs to enter the special economic zone before the new regulations enter into force, among others. before reducing their public aid. The changes introduced have a positive dimension. Thanks to them they improved, among others. the conditions for small and new investors, the aim is to increase their interest and encourage investment. The reorganization of the State Aid Map also contributed to the success of the Mielec Special Economic Zone, where most permits were issued in 2015. Despite the newly implemented constraints and difficulties in providing state aid, the area is looking for more and more new solutions to maintain investor interest without additional support. One example is the Wałbrzych zone, which set up a purchasing group, which managed to reduce the cost of purchasing electricity by up to 23%. The Legnica zone, on the other hand, operates a system of individual investor care, which in turn facilitated current contacts and communication between partners. The Łódź Special Economic Zone in 2015 strengthened Polish-Chinese cooperation, organizing meetings with entrepreneurs interested in exporting to China. I will also mention, as I come from Silesia, how successful Katowice Special Economic Zone has been, which last year achieved the highest profit, i.e. 17.2 million PLN. In 2015, it was recognised as the best free economic zone in Europe and the second in the world. Thanks to the Katowice Special Economic Zone Śląskie voivodship according to the report of the Institute for Market Economy Research is the most attractive investment region in Poland. In view of the above, it must be acknowledged that, despite many positive and comforting aspects of the functioning of the zones, there is still much to be done, particularly as regards the increase in the number of permits issued or the creation of jobs. He's struggling with problems. [[♪ The bell ♪]] e.g. the staggering economic zone. In view of all the facts and data contained in the document submitted, in particular the increase in invested capital, employment and zone areas, recognising the correctness of the implementation of the economic zone assumptions, I would like to stress that the Modern Posel Club will vote in favour of this report. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2016-07-20-sejm-23-2.u228	Mrs. Marshal! High Seym! On behalf of the Freedom and Solidarnia Circle, I present my position on the "Information on the implementation of the Act on Special Economic Zones." State of 31 December 2015 The presentation of information on the implementation of the Act on Special Economic Zones is a statutory obligation imposed on the Council of Ministers. The document contains a number of relevant data, such as location, level of investment, number of jobs and activity of companies operating in zones. The information is based on quarterly reports which the management boards of zone companies are required to provide to the Ministry of Development. The material gives an opinion on the key activity of investors for the economy. Among the 15 leading investors operating in the zones there is not a single Polish entity. Although Polish investments account for almost 20% of the investment in the capital area, the material does not contain cross-cutting information about the size of investments made by Polish companies. This would allow itself to have an opinion on the national economic policy pursued by the Ministry of Development in special economic zones. In addition, the information contains the industry structure of the cumulative value of the investment, which shows that the leading sector is the automotive sector located in south-western Poland. The consequence of this should be to align the mechanisms of the plan for responsible development with the strategies implemented in the national actors of the industry. Road infrastructure should be correlated with the logistics of companies already operating. For the automotive sector it is beneficial to designate a pan-European transport corridor of Wrocław - Prague - Munich in the form of the A8 motorway in the near future. Another important issue is the efficiency of the job creation tasks carried out by the actors, in particular their quality. Absolute number of jobs created exceeding 312 000 with investments of PLN 112 billion is impressive, but currently the expectations of young and educated people are linked to jobs offering further opportunities for development. It seems that the time has come to make an econometric model and to carry out simulations to determine the benefits of the further functioning of zones, or to create alternatives. It is also worrying that the use of all land in existing zones is currently at 65%. The total area is almost 20,000. ha and in 2015 their total area increased by almost 2 000 compared to the previous year. ha. Is this a sign of weakness in decision-making processes in zone management companies? The expansion of energy clusters based on renewable energy sources could be a good proposal in terms of the development of these areas, while involving existing operators in economic zones. The rate of permit issued in zones is 50% lower than the previous year, which leads to reflection as to whether the missions of special economic zones are exhausted and whether they should be replaced, for example. through the implementation of the concept of district technology parks based on mechanisms similar to those used in zones but managed by starosts. This would further correlate with the tasks of the districts in the field of development of vocational education and would, I believe, bring better results. In addition, human resources policy would be closer to local needs. It therefore appears that the government should seek to align the conditions for doing business across the country on the basis of the rules applicable in or similar to the economic zones. The Free and Solidarnia Circle hopes to draw positive conclusions from the information submitted by the Ministry of Development and will vote in favour of the information submitted by the Council of Ministers. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-PL_2016-07-20-sejm-23-2.u266	Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen, Mrs. Marshal! The conclusions and results of these searches are not yet known, but we will deliver them as soon as we know them. The question of changes in the boards, which appear repeatedly, asked by ladies and gentlemen of Members. Indeed, there have been changes with regard to 11 boards. Let me remind you that this is at the disposal of the owner's supervision, this is the responsibility of the Minister responsible for economic affairs. From where and why, and for what reasons, we have talked about this many times. After twenty-six years of operation of special economic zones - and this has also occurred in several speeches from the Sejm tribune - a certain revision of the functioning of the zones needs to be carried out. What else were these zones at the beginning, when they were created and when the essence was to attract every workplace, and it was very important. Today not only the number of jobs is important, but also their quality, so perhaps we should also apply other criteria of attraction. This was also the case in a few of your statements. This is also why a developmental revision is planned. If you ask about quality and qualifications, each new person on the boards has had a job interview, examination of competence and management capacity.
