If you wish, I can provide the CVs of all those currently managing in special economic zones. I think that in terms of competence, we have nothing to be ashamed of, the competences are high, and we will be able to assess the way we operate, I think, in a year's time when we will report on the activities of special economic zones. A plan for responsible development and special economic zones. A question has been asked many times about clustering or concentrating a certain type of investment. The Responsible Development Plan has repeatedly identified elements of concentration in certain sectors, those sectors that are already high in the value-added chain. As far as the sectors that are strongly present in the regions concerned are concerned, we want zones to only strengthen this phenomenon. In some of them this phenomenon is actually realized in a natural way, in the said areas of south-eastern Poland or in the repeatedly mentioned Katowice zone. Notabene - here's a remark about attempts to change - there were no attempts to change or, as you pointed out, inefficient achievements. No, that's where we didn't change our position as president of the Special Economic Zone. We are working together on a strategy, a new zone policy, also with the President of the Katowice Special Economic Zone. The result of this good cooperation with all zones is also the amendment we have made to the Public Procurement Law, which tightens the conditions for the application of the Public Procurement Law by companies managing special economic zones. There have been many questions about land use. Indeed, the average use rate is less than 60%, and there are also areas like you mentioned: a post area with an average of less than 50%. What does that tell you? And here I answer the many questions that have been asked about what kind of instruments we want to use and why, whether we want to expand the zones or not, aim to make maximum use of them. Indeed, when we spoke to the new management boards, we asked them to provide full information on undeveloped areas and what is the reason for not developing them. Is there a lack of e.g. infrastructure, because it is not always the geographical location on the map of zones, the place on the map of Poland, and the sign that this is a special economic zone, will cause, as with the touch of a magic wand, the emergence of investors there, because if there is no road, railway or any other infrastructure, the designation of a place does not matter. I will not agree with one of the Members who said that it does not matter whether he comes to Wałbrzych, Wrocław or Suwałk for foreign investors. We know very well that this is of great importance. Foreign investors choose the location for their investments in terms of not only the intensity of public aid, but also the attractiveness of the place where potential investors will come to live and live. Thus there are still three or four most attractive investment locations on the map of Poland. We would like to change this imbalance, also creating other incentives to not so much move investments as attract less attractive places. Turning to the issue of land use: this growth you have been asking about is often the result not only of joining and not using other areas in 2014. As we know, such procedures often took place, the expansion of zones was often a gift to local authorities only in order to expand the zone without the need to attract investment. On the other hand, to a large extent, in most zones, these unused areas are due to the fact that the areas incorporated into the zones many years ago are practically non-investmental today. Why? Because they are located along the border strip or under a large electrical traction. One of your fellow Members asked about the Warmia-Mazury zone. The Warmia and Mazury zone has a rather high degree of non-use, in this zone most of the land is unused, these are non-investment land. The next question concerned the number of requests for enlargement. I'm sorry, I've already answered that question on the committee, and I don't have that table on me today. At the moment, this is twenty-six proposals, because in some zones it is not one proposal, including more than half of them being proposals, whose enlargement lasts 2.5 years. I, when I came to the ministry, said that if this administrative procedure lasts more than six months, it just doesn't make sense, that means we keep seven floors of administration in an incomprehensible way for me. At the moment we have set ourselves such goals, we are conducting our own internal procedural audit, where there is a problem. Surely one of these problems is the question of the availability of geodetic data by the main surveyor of the country. Furthermore, the amendment introduced by the Law on the Re-sharing of Public Data, the Act adopted by the Sejm in June this year, at the last moment of the legislative process, by the main surveyor who excluded geodesia from the obligation to make this re-sharing available, also has a major impact on the length of this procedure. The thickness and density of the binder for enlargement processes mostly concerns the transmission of geodetic maps and revisions on geodetic maps. Although the main surveyor of the country spent over PLN 50 million for this purpose in the last 8 years, this data is not available and this flow of information is not as... That's an excellent extension of the procedure. We set ourselves the goal of wanting to do this as soon as possible, because this is a process... As I say, in the case of zones, this is one of the main reasons why this process takes so long. However, the availability of geodetic data in general, especially if collected for public money, seems fundamental to the development of the economy and in other areas for re-marketing. So that the acceleration of these processes I put myself, as the person who oversees the special economic zones, as a personal point of honor. Clusters and education - this is what the Member asked here. As far as clusters are concerned, I have already answered that question. At the moment we are also in the process of identifying the so-called. national key clusters. We hope that some of the zones will also be in these clusters. Just qualifying for such a category, as we know, also offers some opportunities to finance investments from special programmes, from the Operational Programme of Intelligent Development. On the other hand, as far as education is concerned, I also answered this question at the committee meeting. In our opinion... We are currently reforming vocational education in such a way that vocational education responds to the real needs of the market. That's one thing. Secondly, to ensure that good, high-quality vocational education, with special professional qualifications after graduation, is as attractive a career path as an academic career. On the other hand, it does not seem appropriate to allocate this task to special zones in a special way as a professional education, which does not mean that it cannot happen in a special area. With regard to such practices mentioned by the Member - just again south-eastern Poland, where we have a very strong clustering, good cooperation with regional government, building vocational classes and schools, cooperation with vocational schools - we are very much in favour of not giving this to constitutionally, statutory special economic zones. Will there be new investments and new enlargements in the perspective of 2026? I hope that soon I will be pleased to present to you the assumptions of the so-called. the new zone policy and their first proposals. We've been analyzing what's going on with our neighbors. As for the zone instrument, on the one hand, as you say, it would be best to transform the whole of Poland into a special economic zone, and I hope that, thanks to other legislative changes concerning the business concessions, we will come closer to this ideal, but at the same time, looking at the conditions and knowing that it is not possible in a simple way, we must observe - we are not an island, we know that we are operating in a certain economic area, very clearly dependent - we need to look at what is happening with our neighbours and what kind of attracting investment they are applying. We are looking at our southern neighbours and the Baltic countries. There the application of public intensity is not in many cases a zone mechanism such as in Poland, i.e. in geographically closed territories, but it is flexible, adapted to data. If an area meets certain conditions that meet the criteria for granting public aid, this public aid is simply dedicated and extended there, it is not closed temporarily. Recently adopted in the Baltic States, aid measures already exist today, and it is assumed that in the case of investments which are currently being received and provided with public aid, it is known for how long it is being adopted, i.e. there is no mechanism to close public aid until 2025, only naturally it is extended, until 2030, 2035, so that the investor has predictable conditions. And we also want to ensure such predictable conditions. This is for this magical year 2026, which today means a certain horizon of operation. This law will have to change, because already today, referring to the first information I told you about the criteria, whether today's first criterion should be the number of jobs created... This is the case, ladies and gentlemen, whether we like it or not: the industry is changing, digitising and the number of jobs is not increasing, and it is often decreasing, although production volumes are not decreasing, and there is such geographical closure in the zones. We are dealing with situations today that we have investors who are in two special zones and therefore operate on the basis of two zone permits granted in these two zones, and want to restructure their production by moving part from one zone to the other. Article 37 (1) (a) and (b) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 1 and Article 1 Article 40 (1) (a) and (b) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 1 of the Rules of Procedure of the Sejm, after consulting the Sejm Bureau, sent the above draft law to the Committee on Social Policy and Family Policy for first reading on 4 July 2016. The Committee on Social Policy and Family Policy, following its first reading and consideration at its meetings on 19 and 20 July 2016, invites the High Sejm to approve the attached draft law. Allow me to add that this law extends the possibility of combining family support and foster care facilities and social assistance organisations. It extends this possibility in relation to the Act of 25 June 2015, also taking into account that these links do not jeopardise the conditions under which children are raised in foster care. The second issue of the law is the extension of the period of possibility of adapting its functioning to the provisions of the Act by care and education institutions, regional care and therapy institutions and intervention pre-adoption centres which do not comply with the requirements of the article. 106 of the Act and were created before the entry into force of this Act. That period shall be extended until 31 December 2019. During the work of the Commission on the bill, the draft annexed to this printing was adopted by 16 votes, with 11 abstentions. The Commission shall, in accordance with Article. Article 43 (1) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 3 of the Rules of Procedure of the Sejm, shall submit the following proposals at the request of the applicants: the first request by Mrs Chcaj, which concerns the way the various organisational units are merged, and the two amendments by Mrs Kochan, which relate to the method of remuneration and the allowances and benefits to cover the cost of living of the child or the person in the foster family or care institution of the family type. ParlaMint-PL_2016-07-20-sejm-23-2.u364	Mr. Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! I have the honour to present the position of the Parliamentary Club Law and Justice in relation to the draft law amending the law on family support and foster care, the Social Assistance Act and the Act on amending the law on municipal self-government and some other laws on sejm printing No. 679. The contested bill will allow for the joint service of the organizational units supporting the family and the foster care system and the organizational units of the social assistance system. The administrative centres operating before 1 January 2016 to support day-care facilities and institutional units have been liquidated, and in fact they have only been allowed to operate on existing terms for up to 12 months. In this respect, the proposal provides that administrative centres will be able to continue to operate on the basis of the rules in force before 1 January 2016 and not only for 12 months. The proposed law also provides for a limitation on the merger of social assistance organisational units. At the level of the municipality, it will be possible to combine the social assistance centre with the support centre, excluding the support centre for people with mental disorders. At the level of the county, it will be possible to connect the district help centre with the crisis intervention centre. The proposed law prohibits the placement on one ground property or in one building of a care and education facility, e.g. with the sober room. Similarly to these solutions, the possibility of a social assistance unit located in one building has been restricted. It should be pointed out that these are individuals who provide 24-hour care. At the same time, it is proposed to extend the transitional periods to adapt to the new requirements. The aim of the regulations contained in the project is above all to provide the best possible care for children, so benefiting from family support units, i.e. daily support facilities, as well as those placed in foster care, as well as those staying in social assistance units. Connecting individuals that are completely different can lead to a situation in which their basic tasks will not be carried out. The proposed regulations reflect disturbing signals coming from local authorities related to the need for changes introduced on 1 January 2016 in the organisation of joint economic and administrative services and organisational care facilities. In view of the above, the Law and Justice Club welcomes the draft of this Act. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

On 17 July in Giby, the 71st anniversary of the Augustów manhunt was held by the municipality of Giby, Mr.Jan Kramnicz, the Roman Catholic parish in Gibach and the Memorial Union of the Victims of the Augustów Oblast in 1945. The Augusto manhunt, sometimes called "little Katyn," is considered to be the biggest communist post-war crime in Poles. In July 1945, units of the Red Army assisted by NKVD, LWP, UB and MO carried out a wide-ranging pacification operation covering the areas of Augustów Forest and its surroundings. Pacific troops searched forests and villages, arresting suspects for contact with an independence guerrilla. The purpose of this action was primarily to break down and eliminate underground and anti-communist troops in the Augustów and Suwałk region. A similar anti-party action was carried out in August 1945 also in Lithuania. After July 12, 1945, the Soviets arrested more than 7,000 as part of a grand pacification action. people in Suwałczyn. Some of them were imprisoned and brutally investigated. Some of the arrested after a while were released, but about 600 people, recognized by the Communists as activists of the underworld of independence, enemies of the new order, disappeared without news. They were probably murdered on the basis of the same decision. The investigation in this case is conducted by the Białystok branch of IPN. So far, the fate of the victims, the burial site, and the murderers have not been determined. Multiple requests for legal assistance addressed to the Russian party remained unanswered. The publication of the Russian historian Nikita Pietrov can help explain the fate of the missing persons in the Augustow raid. He published a secret cipher of the commander of military counterintelligence to Marshal Lavrientii Beria, the head of the NKVD, including among others. plans for the further fate of the detained persons at that time. It is located there, among others. description of the planned liquidation of almost 600 prisoners captured during the round-up in the area of Augustów. It shows that the execution was similar to that of 1940 in Katyn. To this day, the mystery remains the place of its execution. It can be said that the first small step towards the discovery of the truth was taken because at the beginning of this year, IPN historians found several thousand pages of documents related to the Augustow manhunt on the online portal with materials from the Russian Ministry of Defence archives. Several hundred photocopies have already been translated and their authenticity has been confirmed by a specially appointed expert historian. Let us hope that the first step will be followed by the next one, by which we can determine the circumstances of this crime and the final number of victims and find their burial sites. On 12 July, pursuant to the Seym Act passed last year, we celebrate the Memorial Day of the Victims of the Augustow Obstacle of July 1945. In the village of Giby in Suwałszczyzna there is a ten-metre cross symbolizing the burial of the missing people with the inscription: They died because they were Poles. There are 530 names on the plates. It is the duty of every Pole to care for the memory of those who fought and gave their lives for their homeland. We cannot forget the biggest, unexplained crime committed by the Soviets on Polish citizens after the end of World War II. The Augusto manhunt reveals the far-reaching national, social, political effects of the Soviet Union's criminal method. [[♪ The bell ♪]] The areas covered by the round-up - Augustów, Suwałski and the part of Sokólski - were soaked with patriotism before the war, which penetrated into various social and professional groups. ParlaMint-PL_2016-07-20-sejm-23-2.u416	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! July 16th this year We celebrated the 80th anniversary of the death of Gustav Orlicz-Dreszer - a soldier of the Polish Legions, a member of the Polish Military Organization, a Major General of the Polish Army, a participant of the First World War and a war with the Bolsheviks. Gustav Orlicz-Dreszer was born on 2 October 1889 in Jadów. He was 11 when his family moved to Czestochowa. That's where he and his siblings went to Government Secondary School. He was also a member of the conspiracy Youth Union of the Polish ZET. Because of his patriotic activity as a student of the eighth grade, he was arrested and imprisoned by the Tsarian authorities. After graduating from the 4th High School of General Education and Graduation, he began his legal studies at the University of Jan Kazimierz in Lviv. He then studied trade in Belgium and France. Due to the outbreak of the First World War, Gustav Orlicz-Dreszer was mobilized to the Russian army as lieutenant of the cavalry reserve. He's been assigned to Squadron Three 14. The Mitaw Huzar regiment. He deserted and joined Belina's squad and then recharged ranks 1. Regiment of Ulans of Polish Legions and 1. The Legion Infantry regiment. After the oath crisis, he was interned and released only in 1918. After the end of the war, Gustav Orlicz-Dreszer was appointed commander of the military district in Chelm. At that time, he managed to disarm the Austrian garrison stationed there and ordered a draft to 1. Szwoleżerów Regiment and 35. The infantry regiment. He also participated in the Polish-Bolshevik war. After the Battle of Warsaw, he was appointed lieutenant and then promoted to the rank of Brigadier General. In November 1925 Orlicz-Dreszer presided over a delegation of senior officers who encouraged Józef Piłsudski to bring political order in the country. During the very coup of May Orlicz, he commanded the troops of the marshal who went to Warsaw. When the Sanctification took over, Orlicz-Dreszer became involved in the development of the maritime economy. In 1930, he became President of the Board of the Main Maritime and Colonial League. On his initiative, the Maritime Defence Fund was established, which was eventually transformed into the National Defence Fund. His job was to fund the country's defense. The decision of the President of the Republic of Poland was the re-established position of the Air Defence Officer of the State. General Gustav Orlicz-Dreszer was a friend of the youth, which was expressed in support of scouts' sailing teams. Orlicz felt that in children and young people, an entrepreneurial, courageous and loving attitude should be shaped. Gustav Orlicz-Dreszer died tragically on 16 July 1936 in the crash of the RWD-9 aircraft over Gdynia. He boarded his ship to greet his returning wife Elwina from the United States. The machine, which was flown by the general and two other officers, fell into the waters of the Gulf of Gdansk. Although rescue operations were carried out immediately, no one was rescued. The reason for this terrible tragedy has not yet been clearly established. General Orlicz-Dreszer was buried in Gdynia at the Navy Cemetery. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-PL_2016-07-20-sejm-23-2.u419	Thank you, sir. Please take the floor of Mr Krzysztof Głuchowski.
ParlaMint-PL_2016-07-20-sejm-23-2.u420	Thank you. Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! On behalf of witnesses and enthusiasts of history, especially because of the merit of the World Association of National Army Soldiers in Łukow and the circle in Trzebieszów in commemorating historical truth, I would like to mention and commemorate the tragic battle of Gręzówka which took place exactly 72 years ago. On July 20, 1944 between Gręzówka and the Kryńszczak grove during the execution of combat tasks separate branch 1. Battalion 35. Infantry Regiment of the Home Army of Jata, mostly from Kedywy Capt. Ostoja - Wenceslas Rejmaka, in the strength of 64 soldiers under the command of Lt. Zbigniew - Felix Bary, fought a fierce battle with the Nazi invader. The purpose of the action was to enslave the commander of the Waffen SS division. The Germans, in possession of information about the intention of the action, set up an ambush. Twenty-five soldiers of the Home Army were killed in an unequal fight. The wounded Germans were killed at the battlefield, among others. a young, 22-year-old paramedic hanging from the tree - Jadwiga Szulc-Holnicka. Many of them were from Łukow, from Trzebieszów and surroundings, including former scouts and middle school students from Łukow and students of Warsaw universities. This event commemorates the monument erected by the road of Luków - Siedlce. There's a full list of those who died on the monument. Their graves are located in the cemetery in Lukow and in Trzebieszów. These events took place 3 days before the liberation of Łukow and on the eve of the start of the operation of the Storm and the outbreak of the Warsaw Uprising. This action, even perfidiously used by the post-war regime to fight the independence underground, has been evaluated differently. Thus we read in the recollections of death, an extract from the command: The bad fate interrupted their young lives, the sacrifice of life and the blood of so many brave soldiers was complete - a great sacrifice, very striking to us, the others. The hearts of all the soldiers of their fellow troops were covered with great mourning, and the heart of their captain was torn apart with great pain and regret. And with this pain, the hearts of the entire society of the district of Archiv, because we are all soldiers of the Home Army, and we find it difficult to accept that they, the best of us, left us so quickly and so forever. True, deep grief did not break us, did not scare us, on the contrary - they left us a clear and clear way how to live, work and die for the country. Let this holy Polish land be light to the bright, crystal characters. Hello to their memory. That's the soldier's bottom, that's the soldier's fate. The families of fallen soldiers remained pain and mourning condolences. The fallen were not forgotten by the comrades of the weapons. At the initiative of the World Union, the Soldiers of the National Army have their collective graves in Łukow and in Trzebieszów. Anniversary celebrations are held every July at these graves and at the place of battle. As descendants of people fighting for the freedom and independence of our homeland, we owe them memory and honor for the sacrifice of blood and life they gave in the field of glory. Thank you for your attention. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2016-07-20-sejm-23-2.u422	In Poland there is a holiday period, many compatriots take vacations, traveling to different parts of the world, admiring foreign places and cultures. But according to the proverb you praise, you do not know yours and Poland is a country full of interesting places. With this statement I encourage everyone who is looking for peace, fresh air, green forest, clean river, natural nature. I encourage those who want to rest away from the city noise, in peace and quiet. I encourage you to take advantage of the offer of Podkarpacie as a region in many places almost virgin, or at least one in which human interference in nature is not so visible. I encourage you to use Podkarpackie agrotourism farms located in picturesque lands. Farm tourism is an evolving form of tourism, by some it is called tourism of tomorrow. Its development is due to the fact that more and more people from cities want to rest in the open air, away from noise, noise and pollution. This is also due to the fact that agrotourism services are much cheaper than other tourism services. Another reason for the development of agrotourism is the desire to make a profit by villagers who establish agrotourism farms, treating them as a source of income. Farm tourism is more popular in areas with special tourist attractiveness, but it also develops in areas that might seem less attractive to tourists. Farm tourism is a form of rest taking place in rural areas of an agricultural nature, based on accommodation and recreational activities related to an agricultural or equivalent holding and its surroundings (natural, productive and service). The area of Podkarpackie voivodship has natural values attracting tourists, among others. interested in agrotourism. This guarantees a continuous influx of tourists to farmers engaged in such activities. Currently, the most attractive districts, i.e. Bieszczadzki, leski, sanocki, Krośnieński and Jasielski, concentrate more than 75% all agrotourism farms in the voivodship. This focus is due to the presence in the area of these touristly attractive areas: Bieszczad, Low Beskid and Western Beskid, which translates into a constant and significant influx of tourists. In 2015, the voivodship visited nearly 1 million tourists, giving it an eighth position in terms of number of visits among voivodships. The number of visitors to Podkarpacie is growing steadily. 2016 will be the year in which 1 million visitors will be exceeded. The agro-tourist's gonna make a difference. The development of agrotourism favours many features of Podkarpackie agriculture. The low degree of mechanisation of agricultural production and the modest use of chemical resources can be used as the start of organic farming. The food produced in this way becomes a regional product, also recognized outside the voivodship. Another incentive in Podkarpat is the Green Velo cycle route, which extends in the area of the five eastern voivodships of our country. Nearly 2000-kilometer trail is a magnet for bike fans. The Podkarpacka part is over 420 km. The picturesque villages, clean air are the advantages of Podkarpacie. But also along the trail, agrotourism farms are created as places where enthusiasts of two circles can find accommodation and even stay for a few days and get to know the value of the region. Such a tourist model is the future, because there is no better rest than the active one combined with discovering new corners of our beautiful homeland. So I encourage you to visit Podkarpacie, not only on holiday time, but also outside the tourist season. We are very hospitable and everyone is welcome here. We provide a comprehensive rest, recharge the Battery and unforgettable impressions and memories. Podkarpackie is an open space for everyone.
